### Actual seams, verified in source

- schema.ts:176-223 defines site identity/lifecycle/partner display names, without a bride-holder user binding; user:92-106 has account identity/state/global role; siteMembership:747-774 has site/user/administrative role/revocation/inviter, without holder or consent custody. These specific tables cannot prove the bride by role, partner name, email or CPF. No claim is made about uninspected external custody.
- siteAccess.ts:64-90 distinguishes platform OWNER and site-admin membership, sets readFinance/configureReceiving for site admin and always leaves requestWithdrawal false. Its query:108-117 proves active user/site/membership, not bride identity. B ownership of this resolver is unchanged; no change is required for the proposed NEW-only guard.
- The nomination draft already pins site/request/actor(environment operator)/policy/account/operation/createdAt/hash/authentication: receivingNomination.ts:795-806. Existing v1 request identity/hash and self-confirmation remain exact (:31-50,254-270,547-565); the authority must attest this normalized nomination input, not silently claim custody over native KYC input absent from this four-path contract.
- The authoritative repository decides NEW only after locked site/session/membership/account/existing-request checks. Existing replay returns at receivingNominationRepository.ts:420; first writes start:434, then:466. The locked pre-write/commit clocks and actor-proof checks are:409-419 and:497-507. These are the insertion points, not a caller-supplied NEW flag or a pre-lock absence check.

### Private port and exact admission flow that can fit four paths

1. After current actor/proof/site checks and existing lookup, keep an existing v1 replay on its original path. Only an absent service-side original result creates the ordinary immutable NEW draft with the existing generated account/operation IDs. Freeze the exact normalized input, draft, actor/session, environment and server policy before any authority await; no browser holder selector is added.
2. A required-at-NEW private read port obtains independent bride-holder/consent admission authority for that original draft. Its facts must bind original site, actor, verified bride holder (same actor for this existing SELF API), account/operation/request IDs, requestHash, exact normalized input/policy, immutable authority/consent/custody reference and issuer/version/validity. Consent is distinct from confirmSelfAsRecipient/password reauth. Missing port/result, unproved issuer/custody, foreign holder/site/input/operation, ambiguity, expiry/future evidence or actor/holder mismatch denies before repository writes. A data-only verified flag is insufficient; the private adapter is responsible for authentic evidence, not arbitrary caller data.
3. Carry a copied captured authority only in an optional internal NEW-authorization frame between this service/repository, without modifying ReceivingNominationSnapshot, authentication audit, requestHash, nr1 request ID or public input/output. The repository independently denies NEW if the frame/private recheck port is absent. Optional configuration preserves legacy construction/read/replay compatibility; it is not optional authority for NEW. Do not globally make old replay require a new proof field or constructor port.
4. Under the existing transaction/locks, after authoritative existing replay has returned and before the first insert, a private recheck port receives the real transaction, exact original draft/actor and captured authority. It must read/lock the actual original authority source (or supply authentic operation-specific validity under an approved adapter), never return the caller's frame or a cached role decision as verification. Verify the same holder/consent/authority/custody identity and original content/validity; reject substitution, renewal into another proof, revocation, altered input/policy or changed scope. Capture/copy values and enforce monotonic clocks and both actor-proof and independent holder-authority expiry after awaits and at existing precommit boundaries. Late denial rolls back the transaction; uncertain commit acknowledgment never becomes permission for another NEW intent.
5. Successful local admission still commits the same UNVERIFIED candidate/ACCOUNT_CREATE v1 intent only. It neither persists a new holder-consent fact by implication nor grants native creation, READY, withdrawals or provider approval. Existing exact collision/replay/old audit/known historical tuple behavior is retained. A concurrent winner with different generated IDs is not adopted under another draft's proof; retain current conflict/recovery rules.

Illustrative private operations are readNewAdmissionAuthority(originalDraft, normalizedInput, originalActor) and recheckNewAdmissionAuthority(originalDraft, normalizedInput, originalActor, capturedAuthority, transaction). These names are design notation only. No callable, registry, proof producer, table or permission has been created. The recheck result must come from independent trusted custody; no schema/data lookup invented in this task can implement it. Missing real adapters keep NEW unavailable while existing historical read/replay remains as before.

The four paths can implement/test the guarded NEW admission protocol with injected private ports and zero new public/schema fields. They can pin/recheck an authority during that admission transaction. ### Verified gaps against the confirmed bride-holder rule

1. Self-nomination currently chooses the administrator as nominee without an independent bride/site-holder fact. Input requires confirmSelfAsRecipient=true (receivingNomination.ts:14-17,547-565), identity/hash include operatorUserId (31-50,254-270), site loading returns only siteId/isDemo (723-728), and newly generated snapshot/operator binding equals actor.userId (740-743,795-806; repository:441). A valid planner SITE_ADMIN with configureReceiving/current actor proof can therefore enter this local self-nomination path; the inspected logic does not distinguish that administrator from the bride. This is a source-backed product gap at local candidate admission, not a claim of a reproduced live withdrawal or approved remote account.

2. Actor reauthentication and confirmation are not separate original bride-holder consent. Snapshot authentication is explicitly tied to operatorUserId (receivingNomination.ts:80-102,283-317), and policyVersion is expressly an internal intent version, not provider/legal consent (:166-170). There is no distinct holder identity/consent reference, issuer/custody, disclosure version or original holder authorization in this snapshot/provisioning record. Existing confirmation/proof fields cannot be relabeled as holder consent.

3. Durable identity does not yet separate administrator/initiator from holder in the inspected financial schema. receiving_account stores operator_user_id plus remote operator/account/recipient references (financialSchema.ts:151-185); verification evidence stores those refs/bank/version/provenance but no separate original holder-consent fact (:267-295). Withdrawal intent/request stores operator_user_id and original account/destination/request relationships (:445-489,528-584). These must remain original; changing existing operator IDs or foreign keys to a newer bride/current admin would destroy correlation rather than implement ownership.

### Root21:42 account-specific fee reader and quote adapter — ONE proposal CLOSED

The audit's item4 is still a distinct LOCAL_CODE prerequisite. Frozen giftQuotes.ts accepts loadPricingPolicy(binding+method):Promise<GiftPricingEvidence|null>; evidence includes the original receiving tuple, policy, verification/provenance and observedAt/expiresAt. Its service clones the evidence, rechecks receiving after the await, checks original bindings/freshness/expiry and appends the immutable original quote/pricing snapshot. WithdrawalReservations consumes feeQuotes.quoteWithdrawal(scope):Promise<WithdrawalFeeQuote>, validates the exact account/bank/destination/amount/BRL scope and freshness, and checks original fee/policy/consent before a new reservation. Stored balances/presentation and payment GET do not implement either pricing port. GiftMoney explicitly labels its injected arithmetic as not approved provider fees.

#### Public native contract actually found

The official [account-fees reference](https://docs.asaas.com/reference/recuperar-taxas-da-conta) documents bodyless authenticated GET /v3/myAccount/fees/ on the displayed sandbox base. Its [public markdown/OpenAPI](https://docs.asaas.com/reference/recuperar-taxas-da-conta.md) exposes payment/transfer groups. Card fields include operationValue, oneInstallmentPercentage, installment tiers and discountExpiration. PIX fields include fixedFeeValue, fixedFeeValueWithDiscount, percentageFee, minimumFeeValue, maximumFeeValue, monthlyCreditsWithoutFee and creditsReceivedOfCurrentMonth. Transfer provides monthlyTransfersWithoutFee and per-rail TED/PIX feeValue/consideredInMonthlyTransfersWithoutFee; PIX also has discountValue/expirationDate. Monetary/rate properties are numeric; optionality is not a zero default. There is no general fee-validity/as-of/account-reference field or outgoing remaining-quota counter in that schema. Promotion examples omit a timezone despite date-time annotation. These are schema facts, not account tariffs or a verified live response.

The web reader could not decode the official markdown content type; a public documentation-only curl -q GET retrieved the OpenAPI, without credentials, native API or Try It. Search did not establish the provider's exact percentage gross base, CEILING rule, future price lock or quota reservation. Those remain unproved, not declared nonexistent. Production would use the existing client's trusted environment/base mapping; production operation/permissions were not exercised. The [official split guide](https://docs.asaas.com/docs/split-de-pagamentos) distinguishes percentages over netValue from fixed allocations; this does not establish processing-fee rounding. Current A payload is DETACHED with fixedValue commission, not a2% provider percentage split.

#### Minimum exact future private source fit: six paths

| Future path, all UNGRANTED now | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| apps/api/src/asaasHttpClient.ts | Add only a purpose-specific getAccountFees observation, the documented fixed path and fee-only exact numeric decoding/owned transport. Preserve payment/balance signatures, original binding/trust/normalization/clocks/errors. |
| apps/api/src/asaasHttpClient.test.ts | Meaningful RED for fee fields/lexemes/profile-independent observation and real native body/read/cancel provenance; preserve all existing cases. |
| apps/api/src/asaasStoredAccountReader.ts | Add getAccountFees(FinancialAccountBinding) using the existing original-account credential loader/context. No new credential return, account mutation or readiness assertion. |
| apps/api/src/asaasStoredAccountReader.test.ts | Synthetic stored scope/decryption/environment/fail-closed/no-write and real injected fee-read completion regressions; existing fixtures/cases preserved. |
| apps/api/src/asaasFeeQuotes.ts, new private module | One small adapter returning loadPricingPolicy and quoteWithdrawal through the existing ports, using the reader plus a required trusted original fee-profile resolver. Reuse GiftProcessingPolicy/quoteGiftAmounts and withdrawal errors. |
| apps/api/src/asaasFeeQuotes.test.ts, new | Deterministic account/method/route/profile/math/time/promotion/quota/consent-port tests; no DB/provider/resource execution. |

This reuses the stored credential boundary rather than copying its SQL/decryption into another reader. Native fee decoding is purpose-specific; do not globally change payment/balance number parsing or create a pricing framework. No C1, money, quote repository/service, withdrawal service, app/index/runtime, schema/migration, B caller or environment source change is needed to expose the compatible subset through current ports. Wiring a concrete profile authority into a real application is a later exact grant, not an asserted missing app seam.

#### Executable private port/profile boundary

The new native observation carries source=provider, exact FinancialAccountBinding, earliest pre-dispatch observedAt, private completion bound, and only documented selected fee data with exact decimal tokens and presence distinctions. It does not carry verification=verified or invent expiresAt. Its scope labels come from the original stored credential channel; the fee body has no account identity echo. Existing provisioning/credential binding must supply actual remote ownership evidence separately; decryption alone cannot prove it.

createAsaasFeeQuotes would take a required readAccountFees(binding) port, a required resolveFeeProfile(original requested scope) async private port, captured clock and explicitly supplied existing freshness budgets. It returns functions assignable to GiftQuoteServiceDependencies.loadPricingPolicy and the existing feeQuotes.quoteWithdrawal signature. Resolve the approved profile before fee dispatch; missing/unverified/expired profile means no positive pricing. The profile must identify the original site/provider/accountBindingId/remote account/environment/operator/recipient/bank/destination version, permitted payment method or exact external-bank transfer rail, immutable authority record/version, supported component model/base/rounding, promotion/quota applicability and an independently justified validity interval. No client can submit it and no data-only flag from the fee body certifies it.

GiftQuoteReceivingBinding lacks accountBindingId, and WithdrawalFeeScope lacks a native transfer rail. Therefore the mandatory private resolver must prove the former's original account identity and the latter's PIX/TED selection from trusted stored authority, not guess from bankReference text, current replacement account or browser input. This is an exact unresolved dependency, not permission to build a new DB model. Existing bank presentation does not by itself supply tariff/rail authority. No real approved profile/resolver was found or read in this unit; live adapters remain unavailable without it. Do not enable a provider or configure credentials to manufacture that evidence.

For compatible proved evidence, loadPricingPolicy returns the original complete binding with provider provenance, verified pricing interpretation, canonical existing GiftProcessingPolicy and real earliest observation/bounded expiry. Unknown/unsupported evidence returns null, preserving POLICY_UNAVAILABLE. quoteWithdrawal returns the exact original WithdrawalFeeScope plus normalized fixed fee, stable policy version/provider provenance/verified interpretation and bounded times; unknown/unsupported rejects with existing WithdrawalReservationServiceError(409,FEE_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE), never a zero-valued invented quote. Original quote/consent snapshots and recovery keep their captured values. Verification concerns fee interpretation only; receiving/identity/commercial/bank readiness and action authorization are independently required.

#### Exact arithmetic and compatibility envelope

Capture original numeric JSON lexemes before binary-number conversion. Use a genuinely available platform token-source decoder only after future tests prove original-number provenance; if it is unavailable, fail closed rather than build an unreviewed regex JSON parser or stringify an already-rounded Number. Preserve missing/null/zero as distinct states. Selected amount/rate tokens must fit the existing canonical nonnegative decimal/cents parser exactly; no floating multiplication, locale comma, exponent, non-cent fraction, precision loss or silent truncation. Percent units scale by100 to integer basis points, with0<=rate<10000. Native values outside that representation remain unsupported. An independently documented numeric encoding extension would need a later exact grant.

The frozen model is F+ceil(T*r/10000) on charged gross T; commission is the existing half-up2% of nominal N, and total is the current BigInt gross-up solver. Keep that arithmetic unchanged. A field named percentage does not prove that base or rounding. Card's DETACHED payload is compatible with the one-installment branch only if the trusted account profile proves that branch and matching fee/base/CEILING semantics. PIX fixed-only or affine fee data can map only when the profile proves which explicit components apply, with no active clamp, promotion ambiguity, quota dependence or undisclosed applicable charge. Notifications, anticipation and other account charges are not silently converted into processing fees or declared absent.

Native PIX min/max percentage clamping is a genuine incompatible seam: the existing policy has only fixed/rate/CEILING, and loadPricingPolicy receives method/binding without nominal amount. It cannot encode a globally correct clamped fee or choose a bracket for this gift. The reader may normalize those fields for audit, but the adapter must return unavailable for such a profile. Supporting it would require separate root arbitration of apps/api/src/giftMoney.ts/.test.ts and giftQuotes.ts/.test.ts, and inspection/grant of giftQuoteRepository.ts/.test.ts if the new immutable policy representation cannot fit the old snapshot. No such extension, duplicate algorithm, schema column or shared change is selected now.

For withdrawals, support only a proved fixed external-bank PIX/TED profile consistent with the existing gross reservation A, fee F and net A-F, with0<=F<A. The profile must prove the future native debit/value relationship; do not infer it from feeValue alone or reuse an incoming-payment tariff for an outgoing transfer. An internal-wallet route or amount-dependent payout model remains unsupported. The existing frozen fee/consent freshness ceiling300000ms is only a local maximum, not a provider fee-validity promise.

#### Time, promotions, quotas and immutable evidence

No invented90-day/5-minute tariff validity. Require a trusted absolute validity interval and explicit applicability event, then clamp expiry to the earliest real authority/promotion boundary and the already supplied service freshness budget. Withdrawal evidence must also fit the frozen300000ms maximum. Record observedAt before HTTP dispatch; do not freshen it at read/parse/cancel completion. Check clock monotonicity, scope and expiry before and after every await and before returning verified evidence. A late/ignored-abort response can finish ownership yet produce no quote. A current account GET does not itself lock fees for a later charge, settlement or payout.

Promotion activation requires a coherent discounted component, interpreted expiry/timezone and applicability covering this exact operation; missing/null/ambiguous date is unavailable, not an assumed UTC instant or regular-price fallback. Explicit zero is acceptable only with proved applicability, never because a component is absent. A monthly incoming allowance/counter is not an atomic reservation for a future payment, and it is not the remaining outgoing-transfer quota. If the selected fee depends on an unreserved free quota or unknown consumption/month boundary, return unavailable; do not maintain a speculative local counter, price zero or overcharge a regular tariff while claiming exact fees. A proved quota-inapplicable fixed transfer profile can use its fixed fee; otherwise native/contract evidence must settle the allowance semantics first.

Use a bounded immutable policy version tied to original account/environment/method/rail, authority version and effective supported terms. Do not include observedAt in the tariff identity: repeated fresh reads of unchanged terms must not rotate policy solely because time passed and break consent equality. Capture observation/expiry separately, and never extend the old quote/preview or replace original amounts on replay. Any actual fee/profile/binding change must not retain a misleading old version or reuse old consent as authority. Existing snapshots remain the storage mechanism; no new pricing table is proposed.

#### Actual work ownership and future TDD

The fee-only path must capture original response body and trusted platform acquisition/read/cancel methods, not trust a shadowed getReader, decorated fulfilled read, unrelated native stream or apparent Promise/EOF. Observe fetch/read/cancellation rejection immediately; own each actual started operation, count actual bytes/chunks with the existing supplied limits, fatal-decode UTF-8 and validate the entire selected fee profile. Reject status/redirect/origin/MIME/length/byte/chunk/JSON/lexeme/profile failures through sanitized errors with no token/PII/raw body. Request abort/cancel at most once; timeout is admission/abort only. Await actual fetch/read/genuine cancellation and retain uncertain cleanup before reader release/return. Natural EOF and parse success differ. No retries, deadline race, detached cleanup, global realm hardening, cache that hides changed terms or whole-host drainage claim. Legacy payment/balance behavior must stay exact; any incompatible shared-helper fixture seam must be reported before broadening that preservation boundary.

Future focused TDD: documented exact URL/bodyless scope; disabled/no dispatch and site/account/environment/key-context mismatch; source-decimal versus binary-loss/exponent/fraction/null/missing/explicit zero; semantic gross-base/rounding/method/route mismatch; affine/card single-charge success against existing solver and min/max PIX unavailability; promo boundary/ambiguous timezone; quota counters/concurrent exhaustion/month rollover unavailable; approved-profile absence/expiry/commercial refusal; original tuples copied before awaits; earliest clock/delayed completion/backward clock; same terms stable version and changed terms invalidate consent; original quote/withdrawal replay unchanged; actual native EOF/body provenance, oversize/UTF-8/JSON/error, late ignored abort and independently pending read/cancel, rejected or forged cancellation retaining uncertainty with no live I/O. Positive simulated policies are labeled fixtures under existing sandbox/demo rules, not approved real tariffs. No tests/probes/typechecks were run here.

#### Verified current contract and affected seams

packages/contracts/src/internalGifts.ts currently exports publicGiftCatalogResponseSchema as the strict unpaged {gifts,mode}; its array has no item ceiling or byte bound. Public text inherits title120, description2000 and alt240 UTF-16 code-unit limits; safeImageUrl requires HTTPS without user/password/query/fragment but has no length ceiling. giftReorderInputSchema limits one reorder command to200 unique IDs, which does not enforce stored catalog capacity. Existing giftCreateInputSchema, giftItemSchema and quote/public-item consumers share fields; tightening those globally would affect unrelated accepted contracts.

apps/api/src/gifts.ts reads every PUBLISHED row ordered by sortOrder then id, hydrates their media and returns the full public array. Its creation path already owns the site's existing mutation lock but does not count capacity. Archived rows are excluded only from current reorder ordering; admin listSiteGifts reads all states. giftsHttp.ts ignores public catalog query parameters and serializes the old response. WeddingGiftsApi.getCatalog() in packages/wedding-features/src/gifts.ts takes no query, reads response.json() without a catalog byte cap and parses the old schema. GiftRegistry.tsx refreshes that whole catalog and GiftCatalogView.tsx renders all supplied cards. Admin GiftCatalogClient.list()/reorder() use the separate giftCatalogResponseSchema; those response contracts need not be replaced by public paging. packages/contracts/src/index.ts already export-stars internalGifts; no new barrel edit is required.

These are current source observations, not DB cardinality, query performance, browser or deployed-client evidence. Frozen source reads were limited to this C1 and directly relevant producer/client/caller seams. B alone proposes its server/UI implementation; A does not duplicate that work or infer a source grant.

#### Selected minimum public query and paging design

Add giftCatalogLimits, publicGiftCatalogQuerySchema and publicGiftCatalogPageResponseSchema, plus their inferred query/response types, only in the existing C1 pair after a separate grant. No cursor registry, version token, new table, manifest or lifecycle framework. Use the existing public GET /v1/public/sites/:siteId/gifts route with exactly optional page and q parameters. Fixed pageSize20, default page1, hard page range1..10, and max200 gifts in the list. No variable limit/pageSize parameter or client-side slicing of a downloaded full catalog.

HTTP raw admission must reject unknown or repeated parameters, malformed percent/UTF-8 encoding, arrays, blank page, noncanonical decimal page, leading zero/sign/fraction/exponent or page outside1..10. Bound the complete raw query string to2048 UTF-8 bytes before decoding. The structured shared schema takes a numeric page with default1 and q with default empty; HTTP performs strict wire-number conversion before passing it to that schema, rather than general coercion. Bound decoded q to100 UTF-16 code units and400 UTF-8 bytes before normalization, reject C0/DEL controls and unpaired surrogates, then NFC-normalize and trim outer whitespace. Recheck both length/byte limits on the canonical result. Empty q means no search. Echo the canonical q, preserving its case and internal whitespace.

Server search is one literal contiguous substring in title OR description, case-insensitive over their NFC/Portuguese lowercase representation, with accents significant. Treat %, underscore, quotes and backslash as literal characters; no wildcard, regex, token splitting, accent removal or relevance ranking. B must implement and demonstrate this exact predicate on the server before count/offset/limit, not fetch rows and search in the browser. The C1 does not require a new persisted search column; backend feasibility/any migration belongs to B's bounded proposal and root arbitration.

Order matching PUBLISHED gifts by sortOrder ASC, then unique ASCII id ASC using binary ordering. Read capacity, matching count and requested rows from one coherent per-request snapshot; media hydration is limited to that page. Offset is20*(page-1). Between requests the catalog may change; deterministic order does not promise an immutable session snapshot. A valid requested page beyond the current last matching page returns200 with an empty gifts array, the requested page/q echo and accurate count/last-page metadata. Do not silently clamp it, return unrelated page1 results or reinterpret it as site/readiness failure. Query changes reset page to1 in B's UI after the confirmed300ms debounce; page navigation is immediate. Static page0/page11 are400.

Proposed strict response, illustrated with an empty matching result, not the current API:

```json
{
  "siteId": "site-one",
  "mode": "sandbox",
  "gifts": [],
  "page": 1,
  "pageSize": 20,
  "q": "café",
  "total": 0,
  "totalPages": 1,
  "hasNext": false
}
```

All fields are required; every envelope/card/image object is strict. total is the filtered PUBLISHED count, integer0..200; totalPages=max(1,ceil(total/20)), integer1..10; hasNext is exactly page<totalPages. gifts has exactly max(0,min(20,total-20*(page-1))) rows for that snapshot, with no duplicates, matching top-level siteId and ascending sortOrder/id. Require request-bound siteId/page/canonical q and literal pageSize20 at the consumer. disabled mode may validate only zero total/no rows/no next page; this schema rule does not enable a new producer fallback or replace existing receiving eligibility failures. No draft/archive counts, private media keys, credentials, readiness evidence, quote, payment or financial authority fields are exposed.

#### Capacity states, legacy and migration gate for joint arbitration

The selected proposal interprets the human's <=200 literally as all persisted gift IDs in this site's internal list: DRAFT, PUBLISHED and ARCHIVED all consume a slot. Quotes, contributions, orders, attempts and payment/history records do not. Archiving does not silently reclaim a slot. This choice is explicit and must align with B/root before implementation; it is not source-accepted. Counting only active DRAFT/PUBLISHED was rejected for this proposal because it would let the persisted list exceed the stated ceiling and would additionally require guarded restoration/unarchival semantics. If the human/root selects that alternate product meaning, the shared policy and B's exact write guards must be arbitrated together, not diverge across layers.

Future creation must atomically check the original site's total count under the already held site mutation lock, after existing capability/readiness/site checks, and reject creation of item201 with409 REQUEST_CONFLICT. Every competing create/import writer must use the same guard; counting before acquiring the lock or only on the client is insufficient. Updates/publication/archival retain original IDs/values and do not increase this selected all-state count. Reorder's existing200-ID rule stays unchanged. No readiness/finance enablement or repricing is introduced.

Legacy lists with more than200 persisted gifts must not be deleted, trimmed, repriced, partially migrated or secretly grandfathered as a compliant public page. Proposed public producer fails closed with sanitized503 SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE before returning any partial page when source capacity is above200, even if search happens to match fewer rows. Keep existing authenticated admin/history reads and non-growing edits available under their original gates; do not expose private counts through public errors. Under all-state counting, archival alone cannot repair legacy over-capacity. Therefore a later authorized inventory and explicit human/root data-policy arbitration is an activation prerequisite if any such list exists. No current DB count or absence of legacy data is known here. No DDL/backfill is intrinsically required for compliant lists using existing columns and site lock; do not claim that this removes the legacy migration gate or authorizes an index/search migration.

#### Public text, URL and actual serialized UTF-8 bounds

For the new page projection only, retain title1..120, description0..2000 and image alt1..240 UTF-16 code units; HTTPS image URL must be nonempty and at most2048 code units, without credentials/query/fragment or control characters. Text must be well-formed Unicode; title/alt reject C0/DEL, while description may retain tab/LF/CR but rejects other C0/DEL. Validate canonical outer trimming without silently trimming a received oversized value. Use non-transforming validation for the new page cards, or raw prevalidation before reusing old transforming schemas. Keep the original PublicGiftItem/publicGiftItemSchema, giftFields, admin/create/update/quote/media schemas unchanged. No shortened title/description/URL, omitted image/card or price adjustment to fit a page.

Set the complete success envelope cap to128*1024=131072 serialized UTF-8 bytes, including metadata, JSON punctuation/escaping and multibyte text. Producer validates the entire candidate, serializes the final immutable envelope once, measures those exact encoded bytes, and sends that exact serialization. A schema refinement over normalized data can verify only that serialization, not raw network input or an earlier projection. Consumer must bound actual streamed response bytes before fatal UTF-8 decoding/JSON parsing; absent or false Content-Length never replaces actual byte accounting. Structural C1 parsing and request echo verification follow raw transport admission. Raw excess whitespace/escapes/extra fields cannot be normalized into a fabricated proof of an in-limit body. B owns concrete transport cancellation/drain implementation and current failure-classifier compatibility.

If a selected row, media projection, count/order correlation or final serialized page violates the new contract/byte cap, return the existing sanitized503 SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE for the entire page. No automatic page shrinking, item skipping, truncation, success with an unknown count or full-list fallback. Individually bounded fields do not prove a20-item JSON fits131072 bytes; producer fail-closed handling remains required. Client rejects an oversized/invalid page through its reviewed failure path and grants no checkout/payment authority from catalog metadata.

#### Verified ownership gap and minimum pair

The nested request helper currently races its transport operation against a timer-rejected deadline Promise. On a timeout, the exported read can reject while fetch ignores abort and remains pending. cancel starts reader/body cancellation but discards its actual promise; the outer finally clears the timer before the raced operation has necessarily settled. A read can finish logically before a delayed native body cancellation ends. The timer/abort flag correctly rejects late results, but those flags and a winning Promise.race do not establish actual transport completion.

The minimum future correction fits only EXISTING apps/api/src/asaasHttpClient.ts and EXISTING apps/api/src/asaasHttpClient.test.ts. Keep getPayment(providerPaymentReference):Promise<AsaasPaymentObservation>, getBalance():Promise<AsaasBalanceObservation>, constructor/options/types and the existing nested request/cancel structure. No new public close, owned-observation framework, exported/shared helper, profile, config, checkout route, manifest or dependency is needed. No caller source change is required for the inspected awaited-read paths.

Preserve the fixed original-mode /v3/payments/{encodedId} and /v3/finance/balance endpoints, stored provider/site/accountBindingId/account reference/environment credential matching, trusted origin/final URL, GET/no-body/no-store/redirect-error/credential-omit headers, one fetch/no retry, timeout/byte/chunk caps, fatal UTF-8/JSON validation and all current money/status/optional-field normalization. Preserve the fixed sanitized AsaasHttpClientServiceError503/PROVIDER_HTTP_UNAVAILABLE, with no raw key/PII/URL/body/stack reflection. Do not promote an observation into receipt, bank certainty or financial-action authority. Constructor availability/configuration behavior is not expanded by this correction.

#### Concrete compatible-signature correction

1. Remove the rejecting deadline race inside request. Keep the existing one-shot timer as an expiry/abort/cancellation trigger that does not throw or reject a detached timer Promise. Await the actual fetch and every actual reader.read promise; the existing returned read Promise remains their completion owner. Register rejection observation immediately when a native promise is started, particularly cancellation started while fetch/read is still pending.
2. Keep actual response/body ownership even when headers arrive after timeout. Capture the body before deadline/status/URL/MIME/length failure handling, so late or rejected responses still get their actual unread-body cleanup. Keep cancellation idempotent; a deadline before headers must still cancel once the late body becomes available. Store/observe the returned reader.cancel or body.cancel promise instead of dropping it. Do not detach it and claim completion from an abort flag.
3. Finalization must wait the already-started fetch/read and actual cancellation settlement before releasing a reader lock or settling getPayment/getBalance. A rejected read cannot skip another pending cancellation, and cancellation finishing cannot skip a pending read that ignores it. Distinguish naturally observed EOF from successful parsed JSON: malformed JSON after fully drained EOF can fail safely without inventing unfinished transport; a done result caused by cancellation alone does not certify its underlying cleanup completed. An unread/partly read invalid response still requires genuine cleanup.
4. Preserve monotonic checks before/after waits and JSON parse and before final success. Deadline/abort or a late result makes the eventual outcome unavailable even if a timer callback was delayed. Clear the timer only at finalization after actual ownership is settled; do not return a valid observation after the acceptance budget elapsed. A thrown/invalid/rejected cancellation without independent proof of actual drain, or a nonsettling read/cleanup, conservatively retains the existing read Promise. AllSettled can join actual promises but cannot itself certify uncertain disposal.

These are local state/await changes inside the existing helper, not a new task registry or lifecycle abstraction. Genuine underlying ReadableStream cancellation callbacks may return void, but the public reader/body cancel method still returns its actual native promise; preserve that distinction in tests. Do not weaken cleanup assertions by treating a malformed fake method as successful native completion.

#### Compatibility limit and caller consequences

Signature, successful DTO content, trust boundaries, failure codes and financial interpretation remain unchanged. Completion timing necessarily changes: timeoutMs remains a result-admission/abort budget, not a guaranteed upper bound on returned-Promise settlement when fetch or cancellation ignores abort. A permanently ignored abort/nonsettling cleanup means the read remains pending. It is impossible to promise both prompt timeout return and no unfinished native work through this single Promise under that condition. A separate early-result/completion or host lifecycle protocol would be required for both, and is explicitly outside this minimal pair.

Keep getBalance.observedAt at its existing earliest pre-GET clock bound, including the backward-clock guard; do not replace it with a cleanup-end timestamp to appear fresh. Keep getPayment's existing post-read observedAt behavior and normalized fields. Existing downstream freshness, authorization, claim/lease and consistency checks must still run after the awaited read; cleanup does not grant fresher authority or override an expired fence.

#### Current public documentation: the precise unsupported seam

The current official [Checkout reference category](https://docs.asaas.com/reference/checkout-1), read2026-10-13, lists creation and cancellation as its available operations. [Create new checkout](https://docs.asaas.com/reference/create-new-checkout) documents POST /v3/checkouts; [Cancel a checkout](https://docs.asaas.com/reference/cancel-a-checkout) documents POST /v3/checkouts/{id}/cancel. Neither supplies a single-checkout GET contract. The [Checkout FAQ](https://docs.asaas.com/docs/asaas-checkout-faq) lists ACTIVE/PAID/CANCELED/EXPIRED and recommends webhook tracking, but those concepts do not specify a GET URL, required response fields, error semantics or read limits.

No documented GET-by-ID operation was found in the inspected official category, endpoint pages or targeted official-domain searches. The advertised reference llms index/OpenAPI-markdown paths could not be retrieved through the available web/public-documentation fetches, so an exhaustive specification inventory is not claimed. No API host was queried. Do not infer GET /v3/checkouts/{id} from POST syntax, substitute GET payments/listings, or create fictional GET response fixtures as if officially supported. The exact missing prerequisite is a current primary GET operation specifying its path, authentication/account scope, required response/identity fields, status semantics and bounds. No fixed GET path/decoder can be selected responsibly under the current evidence.

The creation reference documents billingTypes/chargeTypes, minutesToExpire10..1440 and externalReference up to200 characters. These are creation constraints, not GET-response limits. The documented creation ID and callback redirect are not payment confirmation. These facts do not authorize a retrieval endpoint, a new attempt after expiration or a financial-state transition in this application.

Existing quote/order/attempt/operation/account rows already hold the original scope: q.account_id/environment and immutable quote snapshot; order request/quote identity and request hash; original CHECKOUT_CREATE attempt/operation identity; a.provider_reference and op.provider_operation_reference; and original account/receiving references. #### Selected interfaces and dispatch placement

Proposed new GiftPaymentHistoryHttpOptions has only adminOrigin?:string and giftPaymentHistory?:GiftPaymentHistoryService. createGiftPaymentHistoryHttpRouter(options?:GiftPaymentHistoryHttpOptions) returns Hono plus close():Promise<void>. It captures the accepted singleton's available/readPurchasePage/readPurchaseDetailPage/close methods once; it creates no auth instance, repository, provider client or environment reader and exposes no general read-port, activity-session override or caller-selected financial scope. ApiOptions gains only giftPaymentHistory?:GiftPaymentHistoryService. createApp keeps its existing arguments and Hono fetch/request surface, adding close():Promise<void> delegated only to this history router. Missing options are valid and construct an unavailable router without private dependencies.

Inside createApp, create the history router and register the two exact paths immediately after the existing global observability/health setup, before if(options) and all mounted routers. Forward with app.all(exactPath, context => historyRouter.fetch(context.req.raw)); using ALL only at these two exact resources lets the router reject unsupported methods without falling through to private middleware. The inner router declares the two GET handlers and exact-path unsupported-method fallbacks. This adds no wildcard guard and changes no existing registration order relative to other existing routes.

Exact public transport contracts:

```text
GET /v1/sites/:siteId/finance/purchases
GET /v1/sites/:siteId/finance/purchases/:orderId/history
query: absent, or exactly one nonempty cursor
response200: strict {history: GiftPurchaseHistoryPage | GiftPurchaseHistoryDetailPage}
```

HEAD follows the installed Hono GET transport behavior, performs the same owned authorized read and emits no body. Other methods, including private OPTIONS, return fixed405/METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED with Allow: GET, HEAD and no private invocation; no public CORS/preflight capability is introduced. These paths belong in the new router/app literals; frozen internalGiftsEndpointPaths requires no edit.

#### Admission, responses and source limits

For accepted methods, missing/disabled/incompatible service or closing router returns fixed503/GIFT_PAYMENT_HISTORY_UNAVAILABLE before native/session/storage work. An available route then requires the exact configured admin Origin (otherwise fixed403/FORBIDDEN). Validate canonical siteId and the accepted generic orderId grammar without trim/coercion. Reject bodies without reading them, nonzero/noncanonical Content-Length or any Transfer-Encoding. Bound raw query UTF-8 to1024 bytes before enumerating parameters; permit only one cursor key, no duplicate/empty values, and validate decoded cursor against the existing C1 nullable ASCII1..256 leaf. Unknown selectors, including mode/policy/account/operator/destination/user/session/membership/capabilities/generation, return fixed400/VALIDATION_ERROR before the service. No browser value constructs the private read scope or enables READY/designation/action authority.

Forward only {request,siteId,cursor} or {request,siteId,orderId,cursor} to the accepted service. It independently owns genuine native ACTIVE SITE_ADMIN/readFinance membership and original unchanged actor/session/site scope before and at settlement; HTTP does not call requireAdminSession, getAdministrativeSession, action reauthentication, readiness or provider ports. Native denial, changed authority, source failure, invalid cursor position and unavailable proof retain the service's sanitized503 boundary; do not invent per-cause401/403 from private errors. The separate fixed origin/input/method statuses describe transport only.

Revalidate the complete accepted strict C1 response and path site/order correspondence before serialization. Preserve PER_READ, UNKNOWN/UNAVAILABLE, sourceOrdering UNAVAILABLE, index25/detail50 combined facts and128KiB UTF-8 response bound. Do not turn a failure into empty history, KNOWN/current state, zero amounts, refund or bank settlement. Every success/failure on these exact paths uses Cache-Control:no-store; JSON and problem content types are explicit. Errors have fixed ApiProblem fields/title/code/status, without cause, message reflection, cookie/SDK headers, capability, hosted/recovery URL, private event body, proof or PII. Catch validation/port errors inside the router so the default Hono exception logger cannot print private diagnostics.

#### Actual request and caller lifecycle ownership

Register each HTTP job before invoking a service method; capture the original request signal and service owner immediately, and attach result handling before waiting. Abort before admission invokes no service. Abort after admission calls owner.cancel(), suppresses the late private response, and keeps the handler/job pending until the actual owner.result settles and owner.completion completes. In every outcome, remove the abort listener/release the HTTP job only after actual completion. A result arriving before completion is insufficient to return an HTTP response; an actual completion arriving before a result is insufficient too. Recheck cancellation/closing after both. No Promise.race, detached waitUntil, invented physical cancellation, synthetic completion or ungranted timeout releases the real work.

router.close and app.close are memoized: synchronously stop history admission, cancel admitted history owners, start the captured accepted service.close and await every actual HTTP result/completion plus that service drain. Rejection is sanitized after the owned work settles; it does not authorize closing shared dependencies. Accepted service.close drains native/read/repository work without closing auth or the shared database. The caller can also invoke the same memoized service.close without duplicate physical drain. app.close is the history lifecycle seam, not a claim that Hono stopped the HTTP server or drained unrelated routers. A future authorized runtime caller must stop inbound HTTP and await this close before releasing the shared database; existing index/startGiftMediaRuntime is untouched here. Health and unrelated routes/resources retain their existing behavior.

HEAD has identical authority with empty body. |
| Native denial/settlement | Unsigned/missing/expired/idle/absolute session, inactive user, OWNER/assistant, foreign/revoked membership and membership/session replacement during a stored read all deny without exposing the late result. |

#### Corrected design signatures, no executable DTO

```text

PurchasePage = { scope, consistency: PER_READ, readObservedAtMicros,
  currentKnowledge: UNKNOWN | UNAVAILABLE, items, nextCursor }
PurchaseDetailPage = { scope, consistency: PER_READ, readObservedAtMicros,
  originalPurchase, currentKnowledge: UNKNOWN | UNAVAILABLE,
```

Only safe site/mode/policy scope is projected; native actor/session binding and original account/operator/provider identities stay server-side. readObservedAtMicros is the genuine stored-read clock expressed as an exact canonical decimal, not a revision, proof of provider occurrence or authority. The first contract has no generation, scopeRevision, purchaseRevision, recordRevision, KNOWN, balance, refundable amount, native successor or financial-completeness flag. The index contains original orderId/quoteId/giftId/titleSnapshot/method/BRL/order creation microseconds, original destination version/policy and exact quoted nominal/commission/processing/total cents. It has no payment-derived current status. Original binding is checked privately; missing/conflicting legacy provenance is UNBOUND/UNAVAILABLE and cannot be silently filtered away as an empty financial history or replaced with the current operator.

Each retained payment preserves its local paymentId/attemptId/original orderId, safe recorded observation classification, actual observation microseconds|null and actual gross/processing/commission/recipient-net cents with genuine nullability. These are facts retained in that query, not certified latest native facts; mutable rows do not form an append-only timeline. Each historical receipt preserves immutable effectId/paymentId/original orderId, verified recorded recipient credit, local posting microseconds and source observation time. A bare RECEIVED/VERIFIED/redirect/SUCCEEDED/ledger credit cannot pass. Different actual money never replaces the quoted allocation. No guest PII, hosted/recovery URL or token/hash, provider account/operator/wallet/bank reference, credential or raw proof enters C1, DOM, cache or error text.

#### Read authority, consistency, ordering and retained floors

The human policy is fixed: the currently active authorized same-site SITE_ADMIN/readFinance may read all original tuples in the server-selected environment, including inactive original account/operator/destination tuples. No current-account/operator filter, OWNER/assistant/support/foreign-site access or action-authority expansion is permitted. Preserve original private bindings through the validated original nomination/provisioning record where available; do not infer the original operator from the current viewer or a mutable nullable operator column. A missing/conflicting original tuple yields unavailable financial detail.

The new routes must explicitly use the configured non-mutating native administrative-session seam and fresh current user/session/membership checks before capture and at settlement. The current financeHttp.ts:215 non-mutating branch only covers existing withdrawal detail/context GET/HEAD; mounting history beneath its current finance path does not automatically select it. No activity touch, Set-Cookie/session refresh, proof consumption, operation/inbox/ledger write, reconciliation or provider call is part of these GETs. Missing mode/read policy/native seam/ports fails closed before private work. There is no new global auth/helper or index/startup change in the candidate unit.

Each bounded stored query uses the already supported READ ONLY REPEATABLE READ transaction configuration with batched original joins and complete correlation checks. It protects that query only. Do not hold a long transaction across user interactions, certify one snapshot across pages, or claim a fresh authority check makes earlier facts current. Actual pending database/body work remains owned to settlement; feedback cancellation cannot assert physical database cancellation or permit a late commit to the wrong client owner.

Index keyset order remains exact original creation microseconds DESC plus immutable orderId as a deterministic tie-breaker. Detail navigation uses declared persisted record creation microseconds/fact-kind/immutable local ID. This is layout/navigation order, never financial chronology. Cursors carry only opaque navigation with checked site/mode/original-purchase scope, and are captured under B's actual owner/session epoch; they grant no authority. Each request independently verifies current native scope. A continuation may see changed persisted data; it is not a complete consistent history generation. Repeated immutable purchase headers must match exactly before appending facts, and unsupported/foreign/broken relevant evidence cannot disappear through pagination or inner joins.

Same immutable purchase or receipt identity requires an exactly matching validated payload. Contradiction rejects/quarantines the batch and retains the earlier confirmed historical floor; no newer response clock/digest/cursor/retry chooses a winner. Validated historical receipt IDs form an equality-checked union for the same private owner scope; missing rows, end cursors, errors, partial pages, retry or explicit navigation restart never erase previously validated receipts. Different retained observations of the same payment are incomparable and cannot replace one another as a certified successor; retain the earlier sample as historical and mark the conflict/availability truthfully rather than claiming which is latest. All successful reads keep currentKnowledge UNKNOWN. No new read, absence of unresolved rows or restart turns UNKNOWN into KNOWN. Owner/session/access replacement immediately masks/discards the old private cache; the fresh owner's baseline is also UNKNOWN/UNAVAILABLE, never reconstructed financial success. No durable browser storage or financial marker is required.

Suggested presentation keeps “Valores da cotação”, “Observação retida nesta consulta” and “Recebimento histórico registrado no razão” distinct. The current line remains “Situação financeira atual não confirmada”. With a validated prior receipt and new uncertainty, show both, without replacing the receipt or suggesting money-moving permission. “Histórico de estornos indisponível” and “Liquidação bancária indisponível” remain truthful coverage limits.

#### Proposed strict read interfaces and fields

These are design signatures, not exported executable DTOs. The required actor/access/session and approved dataset identity are created by the configured native server read seam; no browser query/body/cursor supplies them.

```text
PurchaseHistoryReadScope = { actor: AdministrativeActor, access: SiteAccess,
  siteId, mode, datasetId, policyVersion, nativeSessionBinding }
GiftPaymentHistoryReadPorts = {
  readPurchasePage(scope, opaqueCursor | null, abortSignal): Promise<PurchasePage>
  readPurchaseDetailPage(scope, originalOrderId, opaqueCursor | null, abortSignal): Promise<PurchaseDetailPage>
}
PurchasePage = { scope, generation, observedAtMicroseconds, items, nextCursor }
PurchaseDetailPage = { scope, generation, observedAtMicroseconds,
  purchase, purchaseRevision, coverage, currentKnowledge, facts, nextCursor }
```

Response scope is a strict safe projection of siteId/server mode/approved dataset and policy identity, never the internal actor/session binding. Generation and record revisions are canonical decimal strings backed by the separate writer prerequisite below. observedAtMicroseconds is the genuine stored-read clock, not authority or a record revision. All cents use the existing bounded integer domain/BRL; source observation and local posting times retain exact stored microseconds or genuine nullability. Recommended bounds are 25 index rows, 50 total detail facts and 128 KiB serialized UTF-8 JSON; these remain proposed bounds. Oversize/incoherent data produces sanitized unavailability rather than partial financial success.

| Projection | Exact minimum and semantics |
| --- | --- |
| Immutable purchase | orderId, quoteId, giftId, titleSnapshot, method, currency, purchasedAtMicroseconds, destinationVersion, policyVersion, quoted {nominalCents,commissionCents,processingCents,totalCents}; binding AVAILABLE or UNBOUND. Original quote only. purchasedAt is order creation. |
| Index knowledge | purchaseRevision and currentKnowledge {KNOWN or UNKNOWN or UNAVAILABLE, safe reason}; separate receipt/reversal/settlement coverage flags. No locally computed status, balance, refundable total or zero-by-absence. Detail remains deliberate. |
| Retained payment observation fact | kind PAYMENT_OBSERVATION, paymentId, attemptId, orderId, recordRevision, safe normalized classification/reason, observedAtMicroseconds|null, actual gross/processing/commission/recipientNet cents each genuinely nullable. This is the retained current observation, not a complete native timeline or proof of receipt. |
| Historical posted receipt | kind RECEIPT, effectId, paymentId, orderId, recordRevision, recipientCreditCents, postedAtMicroseconds, sourceObservedAtMicroseconds, safe verified source classification. Complete original producer/event/quote correlation is checked server-side; raw/provider references and proof material never enter the DTO. This fact survives later UNKNOWN. |
| Knowledge/coverage | Current unresolved checkout/payment/event facts are summarized independently of historical effects. Coverage is explicit AVAILABLE/UNAVAILABLE with a safe reason. Unsupported reversal and settlement are UNAVAILABLE and provide no positive/empty fact array. A malformed relevant row invalidates its scoped detail rather than being silently omitted. |

No guest name/email/document/message, request/recovery/token hashes, hosted URL, native bank/wallet/operator/account identifiers, credentials, webhook material or raw source proof is exposed or logged. Cache is ephemeral, bound to B's actual immutable owner/snapshot/session epoch; it writes no financial marker or storage entry. There is no export/refund/support/transfer action and no receiving configuration/proof consumption.

#### Authority and remaining product/privacy choice

Recommend site-wide historical original tuples in the server-selected environment for the actual currently authorized active same-site SITE_ADMIN/readFinance, including inactive original accounts/operators/destinations. This is a proposed dataset policy; existing site-level readFinance alone has not explicitly approved this historical multi-operator disclosure. Root must select this policy or an exact narrower original-operator subset before C1/source. datasetId/policyVersion must describe that selected server scope, with no client authority filter or claim that an empty subset means globally empty history. Receiving READY, current recipient designation and recent action proof are not prerequisites for a read and cannot replace current session/membership. OWNER/assistant/support escalation remains denied.

The future reader admits only the configured non-mutating administrative session seam; it must not touch lastActiveAt, refresh/rotate Set-Cookie, create or dispatch any operation, process inbox/ledger, call a provider or consume a password/MFA proof. Recheck genuine native session/user/membership and approved dataset at settlement. Revocation/scope/owner changes deny or mask immediately. This proposal does not grant new auth helper changes.

#### Genuine consistency and per-record ordering prerequisite

Current persistence cannot supply B's requested comparable mutable financial floors. A content digest can prove equality, but it cannot establish which contradictory payload is newer. A read timestamp, count, maximum timestamp, poll number, UUID ordering or PostgreSQL transaction identifier must not be advertised as a durable financial revision. Therefore no source implementation should fabricate a revision from today's rows.

The smallest proposed separate persistence unit is a transactional history generation keyed by site/environment/approved dataset domain, plus durable revision metadata for each original purchase and mutable payment/knowledge record. All semantic writers advance these values atomically with their actual stored change; duplicate delivery/idempotent replay does not invent a new fact. A generation serializes stored visibility, not provider occurrence order. It must include checkout state, new authenticated inbox uncertainty before purchase correlation, payment updates, receipt posting, inbox resolution/dead-letter changes, and future compensation writers. Account-scope unresolved ingress must invalidate that account's relevant historical purchases or the encompassing site/environment generation before any positive page can be served. Unrelated immutable media/gift edits do not rewrite quote identity.

Payment source chronology remains a distinct prerequisite: GiftProviderPaymentObservation currently carries observedAt:Date, not a verifiable native identity/order receipt, and writePayment allows equal observed timestamps. A durable local record revision alone cannot authorize resolving conflicting native facts. A separately selected server-native payment observation profile must retain verifiable source identity/order and immutable normalized semantics with per-component conflict checks, or its order remains unavailable and differing facts stay UNKNOWN/incomparable. Existing event ID/hash proves authenticated identity/correlation, not causal ordering. No Asaas order/profile is invented or approved here. Higher local generation can expose a new authenticated restrictive fact while preserving historical receipt; it cannot by itself remove that obligation or resolve UNKNOWN.

After these prerequisites close, use one bounded READ ONLY REPEATABLE READ stored transaction with batched original joins and explicit bounded duplicate/conflict checks. Authenticate before capture and freshly confirm the actual native scope and exact generation at read settlement; changed generation returns conflict requiring deliberate restart, with no transparent retry/rebase. Separate requests are not an atomic remote snapshot. Relevant unbound/unsupported uncertainty is checked before slicing facts, so a continuation cannot hide a denial. No provider/N+1 calls or FOR UPDATE/processing writes belong to the reader.

Index keyset order is (original order.createdAt exact microseconds DESC, orderId deterministic tie-breaker). Detail keyset uses declared persisted record creation microseconds, fact-kind and immutable local fact ID; this is display/pagination order only. A mutable record's semantic revision is compared separately. Opaque scoped cursors carry the issued position, dataset/policy identity, generation and internal native session binding; they grant no authority. Same generation/revision with changed content is a contradiction; a higher generation without a strictly valid authoritative component successor cannot replace UNKNOWN. Missing rows/empty pages/end cursors establish neither reversal absence nor safe action/retry.

### Root-selected amendment and concrete private interfaces — 2026-10-13 04:33

This amendment supersedes the unresolved choices and stronger reuse assumptions in the historical 04:23 verdict/original proposal below. Root selected all five rules in coordination04:29. This is an executable-shape design for independent read-only review, not source implementation or native approval. Exactly nine candidate paths remain: NEW apps/api/src/asaasAccountProvisioningClient.ts/.test.ts, NEW receivingAccountProvisioning.ts/.test.ts, NEW receivingAccountProvisioningRepository.ts/.test.ts, existing receivingNomination.ts/.test.ts and receivingNominationRepository.test.ts. **Record and strict projection.** Existing ACCOUNT_CREATE requestSnapshot is either the exact legacy ReceivingNominationSnapshot or an exact plain private object with keys `version,nomination,provision,sealedResult`; version is the literal `receiving.account-provisioning.v1`. `nomination` is the unchanged original exact snapshot including authentication audit/requestHash. `provision` has only `provider,siteId,accountId,operationId,requestId,operatorUserId,environment,policyVersion,parentAccountReference,fingerprintVersion,fingerprintKeyId,inputFingerprint,dispatch`. All scope leaves equal the original nomination and stored account/operation columns; provider is ASAAS. `dispatch` has only `leaseOwner,leaseToken,leaseExpiresAtMicroseconds,possibleDispatch`; expiry is a canonical base-10 integer string reconstructed from databaseTimestampEpochMicroseconds, never Date.getTime/milliseconds. Owner/token/expiry are captured at CLAIMED and never renewed in this unit; possibleDispatch changes once from false to true in the UNKNOWN/attempts1 arm transaction. `sealedResult` is null or an exact object with `accountReference,recipientReference,credential`; credential is the unchanged existing ProviderCredentialEnvelope (version/keyId/nonce/ciphertext/tag). Provider refs are bounded opaque identifiers from the injected verified native response normalizer, not derived from parent/local ID/CPF. Account reference equals the eventual operation providerOperationReference; recipientReference is the proven wallet mapping. Wrapper and capsule never contain raw request PII, provider body, apiKey, parent key, proof token or errors.

The narrow receivingNomination.ts compatibility decoder validates the full bounded wrapper structure, duplicated scope, fixed versions, strict booleans/identifiers/digest/decimal expiry, null-or-valid capsule and existing envelope encoding bounds, then returns ONLY the validated original nomination. Legacy exact decoding remains unchanged. It imports no provisioning factory and no keyring; crypto/HMAC authenticity and row-state consistency are responsibilities of the NEW private service/repository. Unknown keys/versions, malformed nested values or conflicting duplicated scope reject. Existing nomination repository continues to reject CLAIMED/UNKNOWN/SUCCEEDED/foreign/history records; projection is never permission to replay an advanced operation. Its existing source stays untouched, with regression tests only in its already proposed test path.

**Frozen input identity and key handling.** Proposed private PF input keys, in fixed order, are `name,email,cpfCnpj,mobilePhone,incomeValue,address,addressNumber,province,postalCode,birthDate`. Only this reviewed subset is admitted; optional/create-extension fields are rejected rather than silently excluded from identity. Local bounds: UTF-8 body at most 16 KiB; name/address/province at most 256 UTF-8 bytes each, email254, addressNumber32, CPF exactly11 ASCII digits, postal code8 ASCII digits, mobilePhone10 or11 ASCII digits, birthDate a real round-tripping YYYY-MM-DD. Required text is NFC with outer whitespace trimmed, nonempty and without control characters; email case is preserved; identifier digits are not inferred/stripped; dates/identifiers never converted to numbers. incomeValue is a finite nonnegative number with safe integer cents (`Number.isSafeInteger(incomeValue * 100)`), negative zero rejected; its exact JSON numeric representation is used both in fingerprint and provider body. These are conservative LOCAL input limits, not assertions of provider acceptance, legal sufficiency or actual approved sourcing. The adapter sends exactly the normalized accepted fields in this fixed insertion order, and never silently adds defaults/PII. Unsupported real values require a later reviewed version, not truncation.

Fingerprint version is `hkdf-hmac-sha256-v1`. Derive32 bytes using HKDF-SHA256 from the copied server-injected 32-byte root key selected by fingerprintKeyId, salt UTF-8 `entrelacos.receiving-provisioning.hkdf.v1`, info UTF-8 `entrelacos.receiving-provisioning.input-fingerprint.v1`. HMAC-SHA256 is lowercase64hex over UTF-8 JSON.stringify of the fixed array `["entrelacos.receiving-provisioning.input.v1",fingerprintVersion,fingerprintKeyId,"ASAAS",siteId,accountId,operationId,requestId,originalRequestHash,operatorUserId,environment,policyVersion,parentAccountReference,name,email,cpfCnpj,mobilePhone,incomeValue,address,addressNumber,province,postalCode,birthDate]`. The canonical bytes and normalized input are transient; never persist/log them. Input comparison after restart derives with the ORIGINAL recorded key ID, never whichever key is now active, and uses constant-time digest comparison after exact length/encoding checks. Unknown versions/missing32-byte keys reject before dispatch/attachment; copied derived/root/canonical byte buffers are cleared when actual owned work finishes. JS string scrubbing is not promised. Fingerprint keyring is a distinct injected private purpose; existing credential encryption uses its existing separately injected keyring/AAD, unchanged. HKDF domain separation does not prove parent/wallet ownership. Operators must preserve both required fingerprint and decryption key versions while unresolved/capsule/original credentials need them; no deletion period, key rotation task or environment names are invented. Human-approved sourcing/retention/rotation and actual provider fixture/ownership evidence remain native activation gates.

**Repository interface and full current authorization.** The NEW repository owns purpose-specific private methods `claimOriginal(context,currentAuthorization)`, `armOriginal(claim,currentAuthorization)`, `confirmOriginalArmForDispatch(claim,currentAuthorization)`, `quarantineOriginal(claim,preparedResult)`, `attachOriginal(claim,preparedResult,currentAuthorization)` and `consultOriginal(claim,expectedPreparedResultOrNull)`. Each returns only a bounded discriminated private result (confirmed exact record, absent, conflict or uncertain); thrown commit/connection failures become uncertainty, not absence. `claimOriginal` cannot create an account/operation: it requires the original pristine REQUESTED/attempts0/null lease, original unbound current account and strict nomination. It pins the wrapper/CLAIMED lease only against that original row. `armOriginal` requires the same live exact CLAIMED lease, commits UNKNOWN/attempts1/possibleDispatchtrue before any provider invocation, and never clears or renews it. UNKNOWN never arms again. `confirmOriginalArmForDispatch` is a separate NON-MUTATING locked transaction that positively confirms this SAME owner's exact UNKNOWN/attempts1/possibleDispatchtrue arm, null capsule/unbound original account/topology, original immutable scope/parent/input/token/microsecond tuple, still-live original lease AND all fresh current authorization guards. It cannot arm, renew, grant a new owner's volatile dispatch permission or authorize another create. `consultOriginal` is private result/custody ACK consultation only and NEVER sufficient to dispatch; it remains distinct from current-authorized dispatch confirmation. This unit provides no unarmed reclaim/lease renewal; recovery without dispatch is consult/attach only. A restarted/new owner cannot obtain dispatch permission merely from an UNKNOWN record/token.

Claim/arm/confirmOriginalArmForDispatch/attach reconstruct current authorization themselves inside their own transaction: site FOR UPDATE, active user FOR SHARE, exact native session FOR SHARE, nonrevoked SITE_ADMIN membership FOR SHARE, account rows ordered by ID FOR UPDATE, operation rows ordered by ID FOR UPDATE. Inspect the complete site/environment account/operation topology before filtering current/operator/kind; orphan/foreign/additional/history/binding rows deny first dispatch. Only the original operation's expected reviewed CLAIMED/UNKNOWN/capsule/SUCCEEDED lifecycle is admitted by purpose-specific private reads. No nominal find/readScope relaxation or fictional exported transaction seam is used. Reuse only actual exported resolveSiteAccess, auth timeout constants, databaseTimestamp and existing authorization evidence/types. Require user ACTIVE and eligible native platform role, exact actor/site/operator/session match, membership ID/role and configureReceiving capability, server policy/current consent/parent/mode match, live production/non-demo, backend verified PASSWORD_REAUTHENTICATION or MFA_REAUTHENTICATION. Validate proof session/site/user, positive bounded lifetime (at most300 seconds), age/expiry/future times; session expiry/last-active idle/absolute expiry use exact microseconds and existing constants. Read clock_timestamp after lock waits and any asynchronous policy/authorization work, repeat proof/session/site/context checks immediately before each committed transition or positive dispatch-confirmation return, reject backward clock and unavailable proof/parent policy. Configuration/policy is captured privately; mutable session/policy is revalidated rather than trusting snapshot authentication. No provider request or secret decryption happens while locks are held.

**Custody exception has its own locked guard.** quarantineOriginal is not current-user authorization and cannot dispatch/decrypt/use/attach. It locks original site/account/operation in compatible order and requires exact original nomination/hash/audit, parent/fingerprint/version/key ID, account/site/operator/environment/provider, owner/token/expiry microseconds, UNKNOWN/attempts1/possibleDispatchtrue and unchanged original unbound account/topology. No conflicting capsule, canonical credential tuple, new current account, renamed token, renewed expiry or contradictory history is allowed. Future lease expiry, current user/session/membership and revocation are deliberately NOT additional custody predicates: the captured original result may be retained after timeout/cancellation/revocation under the unchanged original durable fence. An identical prepared capsule already stored is idempotent consultation; a different result/envelope is a conflict, never overwrite. History/identity mismatch leaves unresolved custody and no new account/key; the captured owner keeps dependencies while its bounded settlement work finishes. Parent credentials are never used by this exception. Attachment re-enters ALL fresh current authorization guards, confirms the original parent/mode/result evidence and key versions, and atomically changes accountReference/recipientReference plus all FIVE canonical credential columns and operation providerOperationReference/SUCCEEDED while removing sealedResult. Account remains UNVERIFIED, capabilities/commercialfalse; operatorReference, destination, approval and pricing are not synthesized. Attachment may settle a retained original expired claim only through this explicit fresh authorization path; it never renews the dispatch fence or invokes create.

**Captured adapter/service owner.** The proposed service returns a private handle `{feedback,completion,cancel}` and the purpose-specific factory exposes `{begin,close}`. `feedback` is bounded sanitized caller state (unavailable/denied/UNKNOWN/stored) with original IDs only; it contains no capsule/digest/key/PII. `completion` owns actual transport settlement, full bounded response body/reader release, validation, one encryption, quarantine commit and authoritative ACK consultation, and any already-authorized attachment work. The factory registers this promise BEFORE its first await/claim so caller cancellation/close cannot orphan DB work. `cancel` synchronously forbids any not-yet-started dispatch and requests supported fetch/reader cancellation, without removing the task. `close` synchronously closes admission/cancels captured tasks and returns the same memoized promise that awaits every actual completion, including rejected body/crypto/DB work; no timer, headers, race or abort marks completion. Caller feedback may become UNKNOWN on a private deadline while completion still owns real returned bytes/secret/encryption/persistence. Dependencies cannot be disposed before completion/close actually settles. There is no generic scheduler or startup/shutdown integration in these nine paths, and no promise of a finite close when an injected transport/DB never settles.

The adapter takes captured original parent credentials/environment, canonical transient input and server-built original ProviderCredentialContext, and provides a single private `createOriginal` completion. It invokes exactly one fixed parent POST /v3/accounts, redirect:error, no retry/idempotency guess; response-body cap64 KiB and apiKey cap8192 UTF-8 bytes use bounded streaming rather than content-length trust. It tracks the actual fetch promise and actual body reads/cancellation, not only HTTP headers. A late response after caller cancellation is still consumed/validated/encrypted/quarantined when actual bytes remain obtainable within bounds; successful cancellation/body rejection yields UNKNOWN and does not invent a key. Unsupported native response/parent-wallet normalizer is an injected default-unavailable activation gate checked before claim. It returns only minimal proven references plus the existing encrypted envelope; any raw provider error/body is discarded behind sanitized error codes. Encryption is prepared ONCE using the original server-owned provider/site/local-account/environment AAD. Parent/remote/wallet invariants are supplied by the captured provider evidence/record guards, not incorrectly attributed to that AAD. A cancelled operation before possible dispatch sends zero requests; after arm, cancellation never resets possibleDispatch even if HTTP has not begun.

Only the same registered owner that committed arm may spend its volatile one-shot dispatch permission. It consumes that permission before fetch invocation; no await lies between the final local admission/cancellation check and invocation. Lost arm ACK blocks dispatch unless that SAME still-live unspent owner calls confirmOriginalArmForDispatch with fresh currentAuthorization and receives positive exact arm/current-authority/live-lease confirmation from its locked transaction. Generic consultOriginal never authorizes dispatch. Lookup absence/failure/contradiction, revoked or expired current authority, expired lease or a restart never grants permission. Concurrent/new calls reading CLAIMED/UNKNOWN only deny or consult, never inherit this permission. There remains an unavoidable post-commit/pre-fetch process-loss interval with UNKNOWN and possibly zero remote work; conservative uncertainty is intentional, not permission for another create. Original input/credentials/prepared envelope are held only for actual owned completion, then released; process loss before encrypted persistence remains unresolved.

**Original-envelope ACK recovery.** PreparedResult is the immutable original pair of remote account/wallet refs and exact version/keyId/nonce/ciphertext/tag. Retain the same object across quarantine/attachment and ambiguous commit consult; do not call encryptProviderCredential again to compare. Every write CAS includes original scope/nomination/hash/audit/parent/fingerprint/dispatch tuple, expected state/attempts, null-or-identical capsule and unbound-or-exactly-attached canonical refs/credential fields. A lost quarantine ACK consult can prove either exact original capsule or the exact original atomic SUCCEEDED attachment; a lost attachment ACK requires all five envelope fields, refs and correlated operation result with capsule removal confirmed in the same committed record. Unknown/absent/conflicting consult returns UNKNOWN, never another POST/key or guessed success. After restart a retained capsule provides that original envelope; after capsule removal the canonical tuple plus preserved immutable wrapper/result and operation reference are authoritative. Decryption remains private, correctly scoped, only under separately current authorization; no later re-encryption equality assumption or credential consumer/startup port is introduced.

4. **A4 — Decide the split and 2% economics.** Confirm issuer and destination wallet ownership, beneficiary eligibility, exact cents/rounding and payer versus couple net values. External validation is distinct from event notification and must match stored operation/value/destination; unknown operations remain refused. Refunds may reverse splits and fees are not automatically returned. Evidence: written policy/liability terms plus verified native flow. [Critical actions](https://docs.asaas.com/docs/como-testar-a%C3%A7%C3%B5es-cr%C3%ADticas), [Withdrawal validation](https://docs.asaas.com/docs/mecanismo-para-validacao-de-saque-via-webhooks), [Refund rules](https://docs.asaas.com/reference/estornar-cobranca).

## Inline media durability feasibility — complementary read-only handoff

Status: COMPLETE_FOR_ROOT_ARBITRATION / NO_SOURCE_OR_EXECUTION_GRANT.
