**Conclusion:** B's zero-schema candidate is structurally feasible for NEW inline-v1 leases only if each asset retains ONE immutable publication claim and complete pre-PUT byte/result binding. Existing columns can carry the new explicitly versioned private tag and pinned result, so no new shared schema field is necessary under that restricted model. Current code DOES NOT implement those guarantees; this is feasibility, not acceptance of tagged semantics, C1 or operational recovery. The existing fields cannot independently preserve content evidence for an old or replaced claim once the mutable asset's binding is cleared/overwritten. Historical raw-PUT/UNKNOWN claims remain historical/unresolved, never eligible for invented fingerprint recovery. If root requires independent per-claim recovery after asset takeover, the precise missing invariant needs one additional purpose-specific intent content-binding snapshot described below; two hashes on a mutable asset alone do not solve it.

**Root02:04 durability arbitration incorporated:** B's independent design corrections now require database-precision lease fencing on ALL asset/intent mutations, irreversible possible-PUT evidence across attempts, SDK maxAttempts:1 and draining actual metadata/output/SDK/database promises. Comparing processingId alone does not fence an old processor after same-ID lease renewal; every pin/arm/observe/associate/close/cleanup mutation must compare the full original tuple and exact captured processingExpiresAt with preserved PostgreSQL microseconds, plus current state/binding. Possible-PUT evidence must survive failed callbacks, lease expiry and every later attempt; an UNKNOWN/possible history cannot be erased by ORPHANED/FAILED cleanup. One SDK invocation must not hide automatic retries. A caller timeout is not native or database settlement. These are required new-source invariants, not verified behavior of current code. Root permits bounded cancellable fixed-target BFF forwarding with prompt early-denial cancellation; API authorizes before ITS application body reader, without promising zero BFF pulls before native auth or adding a handshake/route. That transport correction does not change the durable tuple or permit PUT before the committed arm.

For root's selected same-claim/irreversible-history model, A recommends NO additional schema field/migration and NO new transport handshake. The optional snapshot fallback below only addresses the concrete existing takeover counterexample if a broader per-claim independence requirement is later selected; it is not a requested addition to this slice. The no-later-dispatch closure must retain current authenticated site/ownership/readiness authority before any mutation; foreign/obsolete/canceled-caller signals alone do not authorize closing another claim. Root's eventual source grant and deterministic positive/negative concurrency tests must make the tag grammar, closed-versus-retryable status and all first-writer barriers executable before any operational claim.

**Can existing FAILED/ORPHANED represent a no-later-dispatch closure? YES, conditionally, with no new seal field:** for a NEW inline-v1 lease, a confirmed invalid original/hash/MIME/decoder result strictly before the durable possible-PUT arm may enter one atomic locked closure. It must resolve the exact original asset/claim/namespace/key, current expected phase/tag and exact captured lease, scan all relevant retained claim history for possible publication, and require the full pipeline/DB outcome rather than a caller error. If any arm/UNKNOWN/PUBLISHED/ASSOCIATED or otherwise ambiguous publication history exists, keep UNKNOWN and the original identity/binding; do not close it as known no publication. If closure wins before any arm, atomically place asset FAILED (EXPIRED only when appropriately authoritative) and the existing intent ORPHANED, with original identity/history retained and positive commit acknowledgement. The same first-writer/renewal/dispatch guards must then treat that closure as terminal for THIS lease. Delayed body/metadata/output completions, a resumed old process and every new handler must fail their next arm CAS because state/phase/exact expiry no longer match. A pending/lost DB close acknowledgement remains unknown to the caller until authoritative consultation; no HTTP error proves the seal. A concurrent arm that wins the lock first instead leaves possible evidence, so closure must refuse and preserve UNKNOWN. Missing/corrupt/legacy evidence or404 cannot be used as the proof of absence.

**Closure versus retryable pre-dispatch failure:** root must not conflate a retryable no-PUT failure with an irreversible closure. If same-lease pre-dispatch retry is intended, retain the guarded unarmed REQUESTED/PENDING/PROCESSING protocol and return its reviewed retry status; do not assert terminal no-later-dispatch and later reopen FAILED/ORPHANED. If FAILED/ORPHANED is used for the durable invalid-input closure above, ordinary binary retry/recovery never re-arms that original lease. A separately explicit new upload after authoritative closure is a different operation, not a reset of the old one; this handoff creates no new identity. Consultation may project known FAILED only for a correlated new-inline closure whose full guards/history establish those semantics; otherwise retained uncertain history takes precedence even if the asset reads FAILED/EXPIRED. The current failOwnedRow/ORPHANED transitions lack these full fences and therefore are not already seals. No extra handshake, closure table/column or second transport design is necessary for the selected same-claim model, provided every first writer participates and possible evidence is irreversible.

**Source basis:** schema.ts827–888 has required/unique stagingKey, nullable text stableObjectVersion, existing declared MIME/size and result MIME/size/dimensions/key/URL, plus processing identity/expiry. Its finalized CHECK requires non-null stableObjectVersion/result fields and derivativeKey != stagingKey, but does not require that version text be an ETag or forbid result fields before FINALIZED. schema.ts890–944 gives each publication intent immutable site/asset/processing/namespace/key uniqueness and retained states/observation time; it stores NO content hash or result snapshot. giftMedia.ts268 creates the asset with stableObjectVersion null;374–399 allocates a NEW processingId/key/intent on expired processing takeover;455 marks UNKNOWN before PUT;513 fills version with head.etag and result fields only at final association. failOwnedRow134–140 sets FAILED/EXPIRED and clears processing ownership. Existing lost-write tests explicitly retain intent UNKNOWN while asset becomes FAILED with no result. That protects history, not byte-recoverable original claims. gifts.ts290–309 requires FINALIZED before projecting public media, so private pre-PUT result fields are structurally possible but every status/consumer must retain that guard.

**Required new-versus-legacy distinction, without rewriting old fields:** preserve every existing staging/... key, quoted raw ETag, pending/failed/UNKNOWN intent, finalized URL and catalog reference. A NEW server-generated inline-v1/site/asset/nonce marker in stagingKey is a never-uploaded correlation marker, not an object, bucket prefix ACL or environment knob. Only that new marker may use the explicitly reviewed stableObjectVersion tag grammar; legacy staging/... rows are never adopted even if a string looks hash-like. At issuance the tag represents an UNTRUSTED declared original SHA256 expectation, not observed bytes; after full server SHA256 comparison and decoder validation it atomically becomes a versioned original+derivative SHA256 pair. Use fixed lowercase64-hex SHA256 components and a strict private version/phase parser; reject missing/partial/inconsistent tags. The exact private encoding is for root/B agreement, not an executable C1 settled here. Do not treat an old ETag, matching MIME/length, syntactically valid tag or object existence as byte attestation, and do not backfill hashes from ETags/URLs/HEAD metadata.

**Minimum zero-schema durable tuple and commit point:** after selecting/validating complete original bytes and actual derivative bytes, persist the verified pair and decoded contentType/byteLength/width/height, original canonical derivativeKey/publicUrl and the exact retained processingId/lease/namespace BEFORE any possible PUT. These result columns already exist on the asset; publicUrl must be pinned before publication rather than regenerated from a changed runtime origin after restart. Pair/result pinning and durable pre-PUT UNKNOWN must commit under the same locked authority/CAS, using the original expected input tag and exact site/asset/claim/lease. All native first-writer paths must require that committed tuple; an initial expectation-only tag cannot authorize PUT or positive readback recovery. Once a pair exists, retries compare it and every result field and NEVER overwrite them, rotate processingId/key/namespace, reset the claim or invoke old expired-processor takeover logic for inline-v1. A proved pre-dispatch same-byte retry may resume/renew the ORIGINAL claim; it is not permission to allocate a new object identity. Clear/process failure behavior must distinguish pre-dispatch denial from possible publication and retain the tuple whenever publication/commit acknowledgment is uncertain.

**Restart and same-claim lookup:** select the exact site/asset/processing claim and its immutable namespace/key; unique publication_claim_key/object_key provide that identity, not content proof. For PROCESSING, compare the retained asset processingId/key/tag/result tuple to that intent. For FINALIZED, require the stored original result and corresponding ASSOCIATED intent/key, even though asset.processingId is cleared. Lease renewal changes only the fenced exact expiry/observation on that SAME claim, so the old captured expiry cannot associate later. A process restart loses raw bytes, not the tuple. A claim with missing pair/result, contradictory rows, changed namespace/origin authority or legacy format remains unavailable/UNKNOWN; never choose another current namespace or newest unrelated intent. Consultation must consider relevant retained intents even if asset is FAILED/EXPIRED or an intent uses a historical namespace: the existing lost-write fixture demonstrates why asset.state alone cannot mean known no publication. Preserve all old intents rather than hide them behind an incomplete/current-only projection.

**Exact-byte recovery and retransmission boundary:** RECOVER can read only the original derivative target, with bounded complete server read, exact byte length/MIME and computed derivative SHA256 matching the pinned pair. HEAD/ETag/provider metadata alone is insufficient; stored decoded dimensions/type and canonical URL supply the unchanged response after a no-decode recovery. Neither a404 nor412 nor timeout/cancel/expiry proves no delayed original PUT. A412 needs exact same-target readback, and404/missing/mismatch/unavailable retains UNKNOWN without reset/new identity/deletion. Positive readback still needs current authority and same-claim CAS before association. After original upload expiry, only that verified existing-object recovery may associate; no new PUT or raw decode. During the unexpired original lease, an explicitly requested same-byte binary retry may conditionally retransmit ONLY the SAME immutable key with IfNoneMatch:"*", after actual input AND newly produced derivative hashes/result metadata match the pinned tuple. Changed file or codec output denies;404 itself never authorizes retransmission. No auto retry/readback worker is proposed. The actual bounded transport/native resource/cancellation implementation remains B-owned.

**Concrete reason zero-schema is conditional:** the current cleanup/takeover sequence can leave intentA UNKNOWN/keyA while clearing the asset's claim/result, then assign claimB/keyB. If the only digest/result proof lives on that mutable asset, A's original bytes and decoded metadata are gone or rebound. Existing intentA has identity but no snapshot, and a later object's metadata or supplied file cannot reconstruct A's verified original digest. Therefore root must either prohibit that sequence for every inline-v1 writer after binding (the preferred zero-schema/same-claim model) or request the additional immutable intent field. A new tag in stableObjectVersion alone, without pinned metadata/URL and those writer fences, is NOT accepted as sufficient. Historical raw claims may already have this sequence and cannot be upgraded by inference.

**Minimal additional shared field ONLY if independent per-claim evidence is required:** propose nullable gift_media_publication_intent.contentBinding JSONB, one purpose-specific immutable inline-v1 snapshot containing version, actual originalSha256, actual derivativeSha256, validated derivative contentType/byteLength/width/height and original canonical publicUrl. Existing intent scope/processing/namespace/key remain the identity and need not be duplicated. Asset issuance may still use the declared-input version tag; no extra asset digest columns, new table/provider resource or generic evidence framework is required. Snapshot must be server-computed/persisted before possible PUT, strictly parsed and bounded, compare-only thereafter, and never populated for legacy rows without original evidence. Null preserves historical rows; it does not attest publication. This one field preserves both content and result proof across asset takeover; two naked digest fields do not preserve recovery metadata/URL if asset result fields can change and RECOVER intentionally does not decode. Even with a snapshot, stale claimA cannot overwrite a newer/current asset claimB: retained proof permits correct observation/history, not new association authority. No such field or schema change is requested by default if root selects the fenced one-inline-claim model.

**Exact shared paths and C1 arbitration:** required shared wire changes remain packages/contracts/src/internalGifts.ts and internalGifts.test.ts ONLY when root grants them: required contentSha256 in metadata as expectation, strict {upload:{id,expiresAt}}, exact empty-JSON consultation versus JSON{action:"RECOVER"} versus binary finalize, unchanged strict finalized {media}, and a correlated status schema. UNKNOWN is a projection over retained intents; media is null outside positively correlated FINALIZED. canRetrySameBytes is not derived from404/asset expiry and must be false after upload expiry; no original/derivative hash, key, namespace, processing identity, raw bytes, ETag or credential enters the status/public response or browser durable marker. B's selected two route identities require no new app route by default. The zero-schema option requests NO reopening of schema/index/migrations. If root chooses the snapshot fallback, the minimal shared schema-source paths are packages/database/src/schema.ts and existing packages/database/src/internalGiftsSchema.test.ts; a separately leased additive migration/snapshot/journal would follow the then-frozen head, never edit0014 or any prior artifact. Filename/next index/generation safety and actual target/application must be separately assigned by root. No artifacts or C1 are written now.

**Preservation and acceptance prerequisites:** root explicitly accepts the new-tag meaning and single-claim limitation before source; B's independently reviewed flow must fence every first writer/cleanup/takeover, lease renewal and catalog/status consumer. Later TDD must prove exact original/derivative changed-byte denial, pre-PUT atomic tuple, process-loss/commit-ACK loss, old processor fencing, namespace/URL drift, finalized replay with no decode/PUT, positive same-target readback versus404/412/mismatch, original-expiry distinction, FAILED-with-UNKNOWN projection and legacy keys/ETags/URLs/intents preserved byte-for-byte. Compare schema/constraints and exercise actual PostgreSQL races only under a new verified disposable-target lease; a schema-source fixture is not DB proof. Existing source12/reader pair and0014 stay frozen; prior0013 TEST permission does not cover a later media migration or suite. Actual R2 development-bucket operations/metadata/domain/CORS inventory, production exposure, credentials/resources and deployment remain separately authorized. This report performs only source reads/status writing, not any of those validations.

**Minimal later source grant:** NEW apps/api/src/withdrawalStoredPresentation.ts and apps/api/src/withdrawalStoredPresentation.test.ts only. Proposed export createWithdrawalStoredPresentationLoader(options) returns the EXISTING WithdrawalStoredPresentationLoader type by type-only import from withdrawalReservationRepository.ts. Options contain server-selected mode/applicationEnvironment, a validated positive freshnessMs and explicit Date-returning clock; no db, fetch, credential, actor/proof or provider client. Its only operation is loader(tx,scope). Existing six-field WithdrawalPresentationScope and eight-field WithdrawalFeeScope overloads remain exactly compatible, with no C1/type/schema/repository/runtime/index change. A future separately granted composition may explicitly supply this factory to the repository and bridge the service's full-scope pre-read using an owned read-only transaction; the adapter itself never opens a transaction or configures startup. Disabled/invalid configuration yields a loader returning null without reading tx.

**Scope and output:** capture validated plain-data scope before the first await. Six-field shape is exactly siteId/accountId/accountReference/environment/bankReference/destinationVersion; full shape adds only positive safe-integer amountCents and BRL currency within the existing contract limits. Reject partial money fields, extra keys, malformed identities/references/version or mode mismatch. The amount-less overload returns exactly19 fields: six scope values plus source/verification/provenance/operatorUserId/recipientReference/destinationId/evidenceId/verificationReference/evidenceObservedAt/bankObservedAt/operatorProfileUpdatedAt/maskedDestination/operatorDisplayName. Full output returns exactly those plus the two supplied validated money fields; it copies them and never prices or authorizes them. No fake cent amount, currency or consent appears in a context read. Use the existing service types and public preview leaf schemas; a new frontend/DTO schema or generic evidence framework is unnecessary.

**Stored lookup and binding authority:** perform one bounded parameterized plain SELECT through the supplied tx.execute. Project only the fields needed from existing site, receivingAccount, receivingDestination, receivingVerificationEvidence and user. Join site/account with the exact scope siteId/accountId/environment/accountReference; require ASAAS/current account with its stored operatorUserId/operatorReference/recipientReference. Join current destination by the same site/account/environment/version and exact bankReference; no current-version substitution. Join ACTIVE user by account.operatorUserId and take user.name/updatedAt as operatorDisplayName/operatorProfileUpdatedAt. The ACTIVE account-selected user role must match the frozen domain exactly SITE_ADMIN|SITE_ASSISTANT, with OWNER rejected; only the caller establishes selected SITE_ADMIN membership/session/purpose/proof authority. The role enum check never grants financial permission. Use a LATERAL latest-two evidence selection for the exact site/account/environment/destinationVersion ordered created_at DESC,id DESC and outer LIMIT3 to detect unexpected joined cardinality; select newest before filtering any verification/state/reference fact and never fall back to an older valid record. Every returned identity/reference is rechecked against the exact selected tuple, including evidence.operatorReference/accountReference/recipientReference/bankReference, verificationReference, destinationId and userId. More than the bounded expected rows, contradictory common joins, duplicate evidence identity or equal/undecidable supersession is unavailable. A newest unverified/rejected/suspended row never selects a previous approved row instead.

**Verification and presentation:** require VERIFIED evidence with nonempty verificationReference, APPROVED account/evidence state, VERIFIED current destination/evidence bank state, both receiving/withdrawal capabilities and commercialEnabled on the effective account/evidence binding. Reuse evaluateReceivingReadiness semantics on the same selected facts so independently pending/rejected/suspended/stale fields stay unavailable. Normalize PROVIDER/SIMULATION database enums to the existing provider/simulation output, never promote provenance. Simulation is allowed only for a stored demo site in sandbox outside production; live demo and production simulation deny. maskedDestination is the exact receivingDestination.maskedDescriptor of this VERIFIED tuple, only if withdrawalPreviewSchema.shape.maskedDestination accepts it. Length120 alone, a valid-looking bankReference, account approval or a caller-supplied 'verified' flag is insufficient. Never derive a mask from a raw bank/Pix/tax/account value, guess last four digits, sanitize unsafe stored text into approval or fall back to a generic beneficiary. operatorDisplayName is the exact valid user.name; the profile label is not asserted to be the beneficiary's legal bank name. Invalid/empty/control/padded profile text denies instead of rewriting it, matching caller comparison of the stored name/version.

**Evidence limit:** the reader consumes the existing server-owned verified destination/evidence association and a safe descriptor; it does not independently verify a bank, prove a label's remote identity, create provenance or establish that an evidence writer is implemented. A syntactically valid mask alone cannot prove ownership or its connection to a remote account. Missing trusted stored association therefore returns null, and real provider verification plus an authorized evidence writer remains a release gate. No new column/hash attestation is silently invented to close that gap.

**Freshness and transaction rules:** read a strict injected clock before/after SELECT and reject invalid/backward clock or expired observations at settlement. Normalize stored timestamps with readDatabaseTimestamp/databaseTimestampEpochMicroseconds, retaining available precision and the Date-only output contract; never re-create a precise parsed Date from its millisecond number. Account observedAt, evidence observedAt and bankObservedAt independently must be non-future and within freshnessMs; account refresh cannot renew bank evidence. Evidence createdAt must not be future or contradict its observed/bank timestamps; latest ordering must be authoritative. Profile updatedAt must be valid/non-future and is returned exactly; an old unchanged profile has no artificial short TTL. The captured original scope/configuration wins: never reread caller fields after await. Capture returned row data and clone its stored Dates with preserved precision before invoking the settlement clock, so callbacks cannot rebind a selected tuple or mutate shared Date objects. The loader adds no row/advisory locks, writes, nested transaction, activity update/cookies, fee/balance/proof lookup, native call, evidence refresh or side effect. Caller readContext retains its consistent read-only snapshot; issue/commit retains existing request/site/user/account/evidence serialization and independently rechecks proof/consent/identity/freshness. Existing database driver precision and real contention remain later DB acceptance questions, not claims established by an adapter mock.

**Unavailable and privacy:** return null for disabled/missing/non-current/ambiguous/malformed/unverified/stale/wrong-scope/provider-transport-independent facts or SELECT failure; no raw database details escape. Existing callers retain their established machine-error/reason mapping. The SELECT must not project credential envelope/webhook hash, raw bank/CPF/contact/payment data, email, session/proof/token or balances. Output contains only required private correlation/evidence references and the validated mask/profile fields; HTTP/browser contracts continue to project only their reviewed fields. No debug logging, credential decryption, secret configuration or provider evidence from browser flags. Historical recovery/seal must not depend on this new current presentation reader and must retain original intent/consent behavior.

**Focused future RED/GREEN matrix:** (1) provider-backed exact six-field and full-scope happy paths; assert exact19/21-key outputs and no money invention; (2) wrong site/account/reference/environment/bank/version/operator/profile, changed destination and ambiguous/latest rejected or equal-time evidence deny; (3) missing/unverified/rejected/suspended account/evidence/capabilities/commercial fields and inactive user deny; (4) demo sandbox simulation allowed, non-demo/live/production simulation denied; (5) missing/unsafe mask and raw bank/tax-shaped text denied, safe stored mask preserved, name from current user and no bank-legal-name claim; (6) stale account/evidence/bank individually, future created/profile/observed timestamps, backward clock and expiry during async SELECT, preserving available microseconds; (7) input/config/row corruption, partial/extra money keys, SQL failure return null without leaking data, while mutation during await cannot rebind the captured original scope/configuration; (8) SELECT-only bounded projection, existing tx only, no forbidden fields/locks/network/provider/proof/fee/balance/auth writes, rejecting transport spies unused. Tests use deterministic fake tx/clock/stored rows, imports of the actual factory and existing leaf/type contracts; no real verified evidence is written or DB suite run. Relevant existing read-only dependencies are withdrawalReservationRepository.test.ts, withdrawalReservations.test.ts, receivingReadiness.test.ts and databaseTimestamp.test.ts; executing them later requires the source-unit validation grant.

**Completion and separate work:** root decides exact two paths after this design review. TDD and scoped typecheck/Biome/independent review follow only a separate grant. This unit supplies a missing stored reader, not real READY, fee quotes, native transfer, financial runtime, authenticated browser QA or terminal totals. B's handoff is confirmed: financialOverviewRepository.ts:aggregate rejects any withdrawal state outside heldStates, so loadStoredTotals cannot aggregate terminal withdrawals even if WithdrawalHistoryRepository parses them. Terminal-safe totals and native bank-effect writers are separate ungranted units; do not change them here.

## Withdrawal consent and discovery arbitration — proposal in progress

Verified internal gap: financial_operation WITHDRAWAL requires a withdrawal_intent target, and withdrawal_intent represents a positive-net reserved intent with financial reservation-state constraints. Neither is a truthful pre-hold OPEN preview or no-created-request tombstone. gift_quote_record requires a gift and payment method/commission allocation, so reusing it would change gift domain semantics. The existing private withdrawal operation snapshot can recover amount/fee/account/bank/version once an intent exists, but cannot discover a quote that was issued before reservation or fence a delayed first confirm after missing-row lookup. A signed quote/browser marker alone therefore cannot close the gap. Candidate is one narrow immutable withdrawal-request/preview association with OPEN, COMMITTED and SEALED_NOT_CREATED lifecycle; existing intent/operation/ledger/history stay financial records. Exact fields/lock/discovery/liveness and proposed source/schema paths will be published after independent review.

### Draft A contract for independent arbitration review

Accept B's immutable price/destination consent, current owner/proof, issued OPEN discovery, existing-only recovery, delayed-request sealing and post-commit uncertainty requirements. Do not accept its illustrative DTO/routes/storage as executable C1. Select one durable withdrawalRequest association; reject a signed-only quote, browser-only correlation, fake withdrawal row, gift quote reuse or a new generic financial operation kind. This is a proposal only, with all accepted transport/source still frozen.

Minimal association: unique(siteId,requestId), site/operator/environment, accountId and destinationVersion FKs, immutable private preview snapshot plus previewHash, expiresAt, lifecycle OPEN|COMMITTED|SEALED_NOT_CREATED, requestRevision, optional scoped withdrawalIntentId, createdAt/updatedAt. The preview snapshot contains the exact individual account/recipient/bank/destination row+version, operator, currency, total/fee/positive net, verified fee scope/provenance/policy/observation/expiry, actual verified destination mask and operator display name, and the relevant receiving/bank evidence identity/time. It excludes passwords/raw proofs/session authority. Preview instants are canonical before hashing; consent hash is separate from the unchanged withdrawal.reservation.v1 domain hash and existing financialOperation request_snapshot. A request has exactly one immutable preview: it cannot be repriced or repointed. OPEN is revision1 with no intent; COMMITTED/SEALED are revision2, respectively with exact intent link or no intent. Add partial unique OPEN(siteId,operatorUserId,environment). No row/tombstone hard deletion is proposed.

No second scope table is needed initially: derive scopeRevision as exact decimal SUM(requestRevision) of all retained associations for site/environment, read in one consistent database snapshot and checked while holding the existing site row lock. Each issue/commit/seal strictly advances that revision; indexes support scoped reads. It is correlation/ordering, never authorization. The existing site FOR UPDATE lock is stronger than the actor scope and serializes all actors/accounts at that site. No request lock is acquired after site: mutations acquire their own request advisory lock first, then site, user/session/membership, account, request association, and existing intent/operation in the established order. Expired-other-request retirement is a separate command; do not hide a second request lock inside preview issuance.

Proposed strict preview input is {requestId,amountCents,scopeRevision}. requestId is a fresh non-secret idempotency correlation supplied only when the user explicitly starts a new preview lifecycle; the server alone registers it and owns its quote/account/destination. This is deliberately different from B's illustrative server-generated requestId plus quoteId: one immutable row/reference avoids a second identifier and keeps delayed/repeated preview POSTs on the same lifecycle. No requestId grants funds or bypasses scope checks. Same request/actor/environment/amount returns the original association, including expiry or committed status, never a new price/identity. A conflicting amount/scope fails. A later explicit same-amount lifecycle supplies a distinct requestId only after fresh server scope eligibility. The expected scopeRevision rejects a stale delayed preview that would otherwise generate an extra OPEN after an intervening issuance/commit/seal. The browser may retain this versioned non-secret preview correlation before POST; it stores no price/mask/permission/proof authority. Missing local correlation is recovered from server discovery, never interpreted as no request.

Preview performs fee/account/readiness/display reads outside a write transaction. Its final issue transaction compares the expected lifecycle revision and current locked operator/account/destination/display/evidence/fee validity before inserting OPEN. Only the winner of concurrent first issuance registers a new OPEN; loser cannot pick another identity automatically. No hold, withdrawal, financial operation, transfer or account is created. A provider read failure leaves no issued preview; any ambiguity is discovered rather than rewritten. A delayed preview with a stale revision cannot acquire a fresh slot after another lifecycle has advanced. OPEN expiration disables confirmation but does not erase discovery or release the slot; an explicit seal retires it. No global scheduler or automatic GET cleanup is included.

Keep the existing withdrawalReservation optional service port and extend its reviewed factory/repository with readContext/createPreview/readRequest/confirmNew/recoverExisting/sealUncreatedRequest. readRequest({actor,access,requestId}) is the exact read-only GET lookup outside pagination, without proof, provider I/O or mutation; it is distinct from protected recoverExisting. The existing createWithdrawal domain method must no longer be a naked first-creation bypass: every first-reservation writer requires and compares the registered OPEN consent association under the common transaction, using a server-only confirmation reference outside the three-field domain identity. Public POST uses a strict discriminated union: {action:"CONFIRM_NEW",requestId,reauthenticationToken} or {action:"RECOVER_EXISTING",requestId,reauthenticationToken}. Amount/version/account/fees come from the immutable server association or original record, so the browser cannot rebind recovery. No four-field legacy fallback or implicit preview acceptance is allowed. Referencing the unique immutable request is the accepted-preview reference; a separate rotating quoteId is unnecessary. Response remains strict {requestId,withdrawal}; before acknowledgment B compares all displayed accepted fee/net/destination/version/mode values against the exact preview, not just conservation.

CONFIRM_NEW reads the persisted preview, obtains any required fresh matching fee observation and actual individual Asaas balance outside SQL, and then calls the existing locked reservation path. Inside that one commit it compares OPEN/revision/hash/full immutable preview to current recipient/account/destination/bank evidence and exact accepted amount/fee/net/policy, requires original quote expiry plus current observations to remain valid, reruns current proof/session/membership and the HELD/unresolved/post-balance-effect budget gates, inserts intent+WITHDRAWAL operation and sets the association COMMITTED with the exact intent link atomically. Fresh fee observations can refresh evidence of the same accepted material terms, never replace those terms or extend the original preview expiry. Any price/policy/mask/binding drift rejects before a hold; ask for explicit retirement/new preview and new consent. A COMMITTED same-request CONFIRM retry is existing-only recovery, never another hold, even after quote expiry. SEALED cannot confirm. Unsupported free quotas/promotions or unknown actual fees stay unavailable; no provider pricing guarantee is invented.

RECOVER_EXISTING is a distinct repository path. It finds and locks only the exact original intent/operation and any association, authenticates the current selected membership and same historical operator/current session/REQUEST_WITHDRAWAL proof, and returns the original coherent stored record. It never calls createPreview/createWithdrawal-on-missing, fee/balance readers, ID generators or reserve insertion; current READY/new bank/new policy/old preview expiry cannot replace historical fields. No row or corrupt/unsupported scope returns correlated UNRESOLVED/unavailable without writes. Legacy existing records without a preview may be recovered as history using their stored original snapshot; they never acquire presumed consent or become a new OPEN. Current recipient changes cannot grant another operator an action on the original request.

Seal is an explicit protected command for an owned issued OPEN: same current selected membership/operator and recent REQUEST_WITHDRAWAL proof, without requiring current READY/designation merely to retire no-created work. It acquires the identical request/site authority, rereads both intent and operation, and transitions to SEALED_NOT_CREATED only after proving no financial reservation exists. Existing coherent reservation wins and returns EXISTING; ambiguity/orphan/unknown request stays UNRESOLVED. The seal keeps a durable tombstone/revision and cannot delete/release a hold, cancel a transfer or assert bank failure. Later confirmation of that reference is always denied. An expired or abandoned OPEN can be sealed explicitly even when the original designation/destination is no longer current, so it does not permanently lock an active authorized original operator. Revoked users cannot seal/confirm; their separate actor slot does not block a newly designated operator, whose current account/context/budget must independently pass. Other actors cannot clear an original user's browser marker or recover their command. Re-enabling an original operator restores discovery of their retained OPEN/uncertainty rather than silently replacing it.

Discovery includes owned issued OPEN associations before withdrawal rows, all owned committed associations/legacy operations in scope across historical accounts, and exact sealed evidence; it distinguishes a genuinely known reservation from client acknowledgment uncertainty. An OPEN slot or unresolved native dispatch/UNKNOWN in that operator/site/environment blocks another preview identity. COMMITTED known REQUESTED, AWAITING_AUTHORIZATION or PROCESSING does not require terminal state before another separately explicit new request, provided actual unresolved-event/available-budget checks permit it. Existing HELD commitments still reduce available money. A client error after commit is resolved only by exact server discovery; it cannot promote a REQUESTED record to UNKNOWN or release its hold from HTTP status alone. Root/B must keep original dispatch correlation while allowing an explicitly distinct operation after authoritative known recovery; no automatic replacement follows a success/read/new balance.

All disclosure first validates actual standard current session/current selected finance membership and exact operator/site/environment. Proposed new GETs use the separately configured private readAdministrativeSession port already accepted for non-mutating internal reads; absent reader means unavailable, never fallback to activity-touching auth. GETs execute readonly consistent SQL and never issue/seal/refresh proof/provider work. Existing finance/history GET semantics stay unchanged. New POSTs use standard auth exactly once and existing proof reader for CONFIRM/recovery/seal. Preview first performs the owned same-request historical/idempotent lookup; only an absent request enters current designated-recipient/READY, fee and scope-CAS issuance gates. Historical OPEN/COMMITTED/SEALED retries do not require current designation/READY and create no financial commitment. Default optional ports remain absent. Every response is strict, no-store, bounded/private-free and correlated; after possible confirmation dispatch, any403/409/503 or malformed response remains client ambiguity until exact discovery/recovery/seal.

Exact optional routes/C1: GET /v1/sites/:siteId/withdrawals/context; POST /v1/sites/:siteId/withdrawals/preview; GET /v1/sites/:siteId/withdrawals/:requestId; POST /v1/sites/:siteId/withdrawals/:requestId/seal; amended existing confirm/recover POST. Context returns {siteId,mode,scopeRevision,observedAt,observationOrder,newRequestAllowed,reason,openRequest,recoverableRequests,nextCursor}. Reason is a finite enum; newRequestAllowed is only an affordance, independently rechecked before issue/confirm. Paginated discovery never treats a partial/empty page as absence, and aggregate eligibility examines all scoped records before limiting the page. readRequest resolves a retained request even outside a page. Immutable preview projection includes {siteId,requestId,mode,currency:"BRL",amountCents,feeCents,netCents,destinationVersion,maskedDestination,operatorDisplayName,policyVersion,issuedAt,expiresAt}; no lifecycle/revisions/account/bank/provider references, audit/hash/proof or document fields are inside it. The strict common request envelope {siteId,requestId,mode,requestRevision,scopeRevision,observedAt,observationOrder,state} branches OPEN with immutable preview; EXISTING with original command/withdrawal and historical preview-or-unavailable display; SEALED_NOT_CREATED with exact issued command/seal evidence; UNRESOLVED with original command only when authoritatively known. Unknown refs have null requestRevision, never an invented0 permission. Preview issue/retry, exact GET and seal all return this same request-correlated union, never a new OPEN envelope after commit/seal or naked success.

Masking is a separately verified stored association: receivingDestination.maskedDescriptor is nullable and only length-bounded, not a bank-verification or privacy guarantee. For NEW require an explicitly safe masked presentation from the exact verified account/environment/destination row/version/bank reference and its evidence; missing/unsafe/unproven masking makes preview unavailable. Freeze that presentation in the association before consent. Operator displayName comes from the actual stored operator profile and is labeled as that display name, not invented proof of legal bank-beneficiary identity. Existing historical rows without that verified frozen presentation return display unavailable while preserving readable monetary/request status; never substitute the current bank/profile. No raw bank string is derived or collected from the caller.

Expiry/receiving/fee/session/balance fences use canonical database microseconds and monotonic within-operation clocks; original consent expiry is immutable. GET exposes the consistent-snapshot ordering tuple defined below, with exact stored modification microseconds and request/scope revisions, so sub-millisecond public ISO truncation does not regress OPEN/COMMITTED/SEALED. Equal-order contradictory observations are uncertain and cannot clear a marker or enable a new action. Read generations/current owner disposal also fence out-of-order transport; observation timestamps alone confer no authority. Future native state writers must preserve account locking, exact effects and observation ordering; no such writer is granted here.

This design's durable seals require every executable first-reservation writer to participate in the association gate. The old transport or a mixed old runtime that can insert without a preview cannot coexist with a claimed seal guarantee. Before runtime enablement root must prove that obsolete handlers/workers are drained/version-gated and new missing-association confirmation fails closed. Startup remains unconfigured now; no rollout or operational cutover is granted. Tombstones and monotonic revision evidence are retained; no archive/cleanup can reset the high-water mark or permit identity reuse without a separately reviewed policy.

### Minimal proposed A source/schema scope and acceptance

Review refinements and exact schema names: idempotent same-request lookup/ownership/amount comparison occurs BEFORE fee I/O and BEFORE expected scopeRevision CAS, because an acknowledged issue or lost ACK already advanced the scope. OPEN/COMMITTED/SEALED replay returns that original association and cannot consult another fee or reopen its slot; only absent IDs enter new issuance. Root/B's illustrative extra quoteId/issuanceId is rejected as unnecessary for one immutable request. Explicit seal is also idempotent: an existing sealed row returns the original revision/timestamp; no revision is incremented on replay. Unknown IDs remain UNRESOLVED rather than inserting an invented blank preview/tombstone. The backend still rechecks READY/recipient/destination/fees/budget independently; SUM lifecycle revision does not attest those facts or subsequent native operation state.

Proposed exported C1 names: withdrawalPreviewInputSchema ({requestId,amountCents,scopeRevision}); withdrawalPreviewSchema (immutable display/terms only); withdrawalRequestContextSchema/withdrawalRequestContextResponseSchema ({context}); withdrawalRequestStatusSchema/withdrawalRequestStatusResponseSchema ({request}); withdrawalSealInputSchema ({reauthenticationToken}, requestId only in the protected route); amended discriminated withdrawalCreateInputSchema ({action:CONFIRM_NEW|RECOVER_EXISTING,requestId,reauthenticationToken}). Add their inferred public types following existing conventions. withdrawalReservationInputSchema/WithdrawalReservationInput remain three-field internal command, and withdrawalResponseSchema/WithdrawalIntent/list/history stay as accepted. Token grammar is reused after safe initialization, not duplicated. request IDs retain the accepted local-ID bounds; revisions are canonical nonnegative decimal strings parsed with BigInt, never JS floating arithmetic. Finite context reasons: OPEN_REQUEST, UNRESOLVED_OPERATION, RECIPIENT_REQUIRED, DESTINATION_UNAVAILABLE, NOT_CONFIGURED, CONTEXT_UNAVAILABLE, with null only for allowed preview affordance. Every positive eligibility still requires the same current server-owned authority under mutation locks. A pagination cursor is non-secret bounded correlation; it cannot filter away blocking aggregate evidence.

The common withdrawalRequestStatusResponseSchema is used by preview issuance/retry, exact recovery GET and seal, avoiding a second success envelope. Its OPEN branch contains the immutable preview; EXISTING/SEALED/UNRESOLVED communicate current authoritative lifecycle on idempotent preview retry. The preview itself has no mutable state/requestRevision/scopeRevision: those fields belong to the correlated request envelope. Therefore a delayed preview response or same-request retry after COMMITTED cannot manufacture an OPEN affordance or replace original terms.

Observation ordering is explicitly a consistent SQL snapshot, never the clock at response completion. observedAt is captured at snapshot acquisition; its millisecond ISO text is presentation/freshness only. observationOrder is a strict tuple of scopeRevision, requestRevision and exact stored recordUpdatedAtMicroseconds (nullable only when no authoritative request/record exists), sourced from association and original intent/operation modification evidence in that same snapshot. Compare same-scope lifecycle revisions and persisted microseconds; a late older snapshot cannot overwrite a higher revision. Equal/incomparable contradictory evidence remains unavailable and cannot retire uncertainty. OPEN/COMMITTED/SEALED revision1/2 orders this finite lifecycle, not REQUESTED/PROCESSING/UNKNOWN within COMMITTED. Any later native state writer needs its own monotonic update/version evidence under the account lock before acceptance; no native ordering guarantee is implied. Legacy historical records without an association remain read/recovery-only with explicit null association revision and exact available stored operation times, never a fabricated consent/revision. Missing authoritative order keeps uncertainty rather than claiming a newer result.

Candidate scope is twelve existing files, no new generic service/table family: contracts internalGifts.ts/.test.ts; database financialSchema.ts/.test.ts; API withdrawalReservations.ts/.test.ts, withdrawalReservationRepository.ts/.test.ts, financeHttp.ts/.test.ts and app.ts/appIntegration.test.ts. Existing contracts and database schema wildcard exports cover the one new association without index/schema.ts edits. Extend the existing service/repository as the common financial transaction owner instead of nested transactions or a parallel wallet. No auth/proof/native/receiving-repository/history/overview/B source change is proposed. Any demonstrated extra path must be separately requested. B's eight proposed files remain ungranted until reviewed executable C1 and server serialization tests exist.

One narrow additive schema/migration is unavoidable if root accepts this association; request schema-source/TDD first, then a separate OFFLINE migration-generation lease only after source freeze/review. Its outputs are only the next Drizzle SQL file, matching meta snapshot and _journal entry; the coordinator resolves exact next filenames before granting generation. Preserve old SQL/ledger/history and existing tables/records; no generation/application/env/DB command occurs during arbitration. Application and actual acceptance require a separate approved exact TEST target/backup/preservation/fixture lease.

Deterministic RED-first cases after an exact grant: immutable preview/conservation/mask/scope/expiry/hash and no raw token; duplicate/same-id versus new same-amount preview; active OPEN discovery with lost marker; scope-revision CAS before and after fee I/O; simultaneous issuers and confirm/seal interleavings; site/request/account lock order including timeout/revocation; delayed confirm denied after seal and known intent winning seal; no orphan association or partial intent/op/COMMITTED on rollback; actual commit followed by lost ACK/proof expiry; existing-only missing never inserts/requotes/rebinds; expired preview recovery; legacy originals with unavailable mask; current/new recipient histories; new explicit request after known REQUESTED with correct HELD budget; unresolved native UNKNOWN blocks replacement; readonly GET/no cookies/no activity/no write; pagination and observation-order ambiguity. Fake serial SQL drivers prove generated statements/algorithm only. Independent Sol review, contracts/API TypeScript and scoped formatting precede C1 handoff. Later PostgreSQL must prove real overlapping lock races, FK/partial-index atomicity/revision, precise timestamps, seal fences, lost ACK and preserved original36-table/ledger state under its own lease. Actual verified individual account/fee/bank/mask/PF/BaaS/native continuation and protected React/browser acceptance remain independent gates; no fee-zero or native completion shortcut is proposed.

## Protected withdrawal action proposal — source grant pending

### Verified boundary

Existing C1 withdrawalCreateInputSchema accepts exactly requestId, amountCents and destinationVersion (packages/contracts/src/internalGifts.ts436); it has no proof. withdrawalResponseSchema currently wraps only withdrawal and has no production caller; history independently consumes withdrawalListResponseSchema and WithdrawalIntent. financeHttp.ts121-141 validates the old body then always returns409 PROVIDER_MODE_UNAVAILABLE; it exposes no action port. Existing middleware authenticates trusted Origin/current standard session, resolves selected-site finance membership, rejects queries and protects both read endpoints. ApiOptions has finance read ports and financialReauthentication but no withdrawal reservation port. No active administrator withdrawal-create consumer exists in current apps/admin; FinanceSection and WithdrawalHistoryView are read-only. The existing proof service already signs/verifies the REQUEST_WITHDRAWAL purpose with user/site/session binding.

createWithdrawalReservationService already accepts server actor/access/recentAuth plus the three-field domain command. It chooses receiving context, requires current designated recipient, READY destination/version, verified scoped fees and fresh individual-account Asaas balance before first reservation; captures original fee/balance/identity/audit and runs current-session/membership/recipient/budget checks again under the repository lock. Amount is total commitment including fee; net plus fee exactly equals amount. The operation creates one REQUESTED/HELD intent and one WITHDRAWAL operation, without dispatching a transfer. Same-request replay preserves the original snapshot/state/IDs/audit and performs no new fee/balance request or hold. Existing unit/mock tests encode these guarantees; this read-only task did not rerun them or establish SQL acceptance.

The existing index was inspected read-only for withdrawal/finance vocabulary and connected planning nodes before source inspection. It indexes the finance router and reviewed reservation/history boundaries but is not behavioral proof. No Graphify command or index write occurred under root's reserved ownership; source is authoritative here.

### Smallest C1 and optional API seam

Propose a strict public withdrawalCreateInputSchema with exactly {requestId,amountCents,destinationVersion,reauthenticationToken}, using the existing financialReauthenticationResponseSchema token grammar/4096-character bound. Require the token; never accept a missing-token legacy fallback, recentAuth DTO, approval flag, user/session, account/wallet/key, bank details, fee, balance or mode. destinationVersion is only the client's optimistic match to the server-published verified version; it cannot select or change a destination.

Introduce withdrawalReservationInputSchema with the original strict three fields and inferred WithdrawalReservationInput; add inferred WithdrawalCreateInput for the new wire shape. The existing reservation service switches its parser import to the three-field domain schema. HTTP validates the complete four-field wire input, verifies the token through the configured real financialReauthentication.readProof(actor,"REQUEST_WITHDRAWAL",token), and passes only the three domain fields plus verified backend evidence to the existing service. The token is transient: no hash input, snapshot, database field, URL, log, response or public history contains it. The unchanged withdrawal.reservation.v1 hash and stored old three-field commands remain valid; a fresh current-session token must not change the original operation identity/audit. Existing direct service/repository tests keep their three-field inputs; the old proof-free HTTP fixture migrates to a clearly synthetic signed proof. No migration or schema rewrite is necessary.

Amend the currently unused strict withdrawalResponseSchema envelope to {requestId,withdrawal}, retaining WithdrawalIntent unchanged. HTTP returns the accepted original input requestId for exact caller correlation and validates the real service's public intent: same site/amount/destinationVersion, legal sandbox/live mode, positive net, integer cents and exact conservation, with strict projection and sanitized errors. Use200 for both initial REQUESTED reservation and exact replay; do not label the response provider execution or bank completion. REQUESTED is the only initial result from this factory; replay preserves the stored supported state, including UNKNOWN. Do not make a response state up or accept disabled mode from an action port. The existing concrete repository currently validates pristine REQUESTED/HELD or consistent UNKNOWN evidence; its tests reject unsupported terminal mutations. Public terminal states in C1 do not imply a reviewed native completion writer exists.

Propose optional withdrawalReservation?:WithdrawalReservationService on FinanceHttpOptions and ApiOptions, alongside the already optional financialReauthentication; retain FinanceHttpReadPorts as read-only. The existing createFinanceHttpRouter(options) registration suffices. Carry the middleware's server-resolved actor/site access into the POST through typed request-local context so standard auth is called once; never use the nomination-only isolated GET auth or parse a browser actor. Authenticate Origin/current session/current selected SITE_ADMIN/readFinance first, then bounded strict JSON body, then required optional ports and purpose-specific proof, then the real service. Reject query/private authority fields, malformed JSON/UTF-8/MIME and bodies over8192 bytes; cancel oversized streams. The limit accommodates the existing4096-character token plus the remaining valid fields. Existing finance/history GET behavior remains unchanged. Missing action/proof ports keep a well-formed POST409 PROVIDER_MODE_UNAVAILABLE with zero action/provider calls; invalid/missing proof stays400 or403 according to syntax versus verification. Known typed service/auth/readiness errors retain sanitized status/code; unexpected errors become503 without private causes. Success and failure are no-store.

First reservation requires server loadContext to select the stored current individual ASAAS account, designated operator and immutable verified bankReference/version. The scoped balance adapter obtains credentials only from server-owned encrypted credential context {provider,siteId,accountBindingId,environment}; the existing cryptographic helper is not a configured durable credential reader. No browser value selects that account, key or bank. maxAuthAge and clock must match the explicit proof/service composition, freshness remains bounded, fees must match the exact amount/account/environment/destination and provider terms, and balance must be an actual scoped fresh Asaas observation. No ledger sum, overview DTO, zero-default fee or fake READY is substituted. Root startup/index remains unchanged without these real ports.

### Existing replay and commitment invariants to preserve

Repository authority is stronger than a role flag: site/request locking, active locked user/current session with strict creation/idle/expiry checks, current selected membership, and the account/destination scope precede the callback. First creation additionally requires current verified destination/receiving evidence, zero unresolved financial events, no completed withdrawal/debit at or after balance observation, and provider balance minus all HELD commitments covering the full amount. Completion/refund/bank writers must use the same account lock before changing those facts; their presence is not established by this proposal.

Exact replay is authenticated recovery of an existing operation, not a new financial action. The repository takes the original stored snapshot before choosing its historical account/destination and requires that historical account's operator still matches actor/original operator; it retains the original version/bankReference. It can recover after a later current account/destination version exists while historical rows remain intact; it denies an in-place historical operator/reference change. Therefore HTTP must not require current READY/current destination/designated-new-account before allowing the service to distinguish replay. Fresh current proof and selected membership remain mandatory, but replay must not rebind, requote, generate IDs, add a hold or dispatch. REQUEST_CONFLICT on changed amount/version retains the old request; lost commit acknowledgment or UNKNOWN retains the original commitment and permits only explicit same-request reconciliation, never a new request identity or blind provider retry.

### Separate Asaas execution, critical authorization and completion

Current official transfer reference documents POST /v3/transfers from the authenticated account to server-selected bank/Pix data; creation can remain incomplete and must be followed through transfer events. externalReference is described as application correlation, not proof of provider idempotency; no automatic replay safety was established. A future adapter must map the immutable local total/fee/net to actual provider value/netValue/transferFee semantics and prove available-balance/held treatment without double counting. [Transfer reference](https://docs.asaas.com/reference/transferir-para-conta-de-outra-instituicao-ou-chave-pix).

The local fr1 REQUEST_WITHDRAWAL proof authenticates the panel action only. Provider critical-action requirements depend on endpoint/account configuration, and provider webhook withdrawal authorization is a separate authenticated approval/refusal comparison against the recorded actual operation. Neither is satisfied by a valid local password token or structurally valid provider payload. Their exact approved continuation, account configuration and PF/BaaS eligibility remain pending; do not disable approval or add test-token production defaults. [Critical actions](https://docs.asaas.com/docs/como-testar-a%C3%A7%C3%B5es-cr%C3%ADticas), [Withdrawal authorization](https://docs.asaas.com/docs/mecanismo-para-validacao-de-saque-via-webhooks).

Account fee metadata includes monthly free-transfer quota, method-specific quota inclusion and Pix discount expiry as well as values. Those terms do not alone prove an exact fee for this committed transfer or concurrent quota ownership; unsupported/ambiguous terms must remain FEE_QUOTE_UNAVAILABLE. No generic table, default zero, guessed free quota or approximate tariff is proposed. [Account fees and OpenAPI fields](https://docs.asaas.com/reference/recuperar-taxas-da-conta.md).

REQUESTED is durable local reservation; later AWAITING_AUTHORIZATION/PROCESSING require reviewed native observations and continuation. Provider completion, bank receipt and reversals require scoped authenticated events/reconciliation, exact financial effects/ledger proof and protected receipt handling. An HTTP200, accepted native POST, successful provider approval or elapsed time is not proof of bank credit. No native transfer, operational worker, approval callback, terminal writer, receipt, refund, bank verification or money movement is included in the next eight-file source request.

## Finite nomination-context proposal — decision pending, no implementation

### Verified problem and scope

B's completed read-only audit at internalGiftsRegistryStatus.md108-128 is corroborated in current source: MeResponse already includes user.id/session.id/memberships; /v1/me and requireAdminSession update lastActiveAt; receivingNominationClient requires a caller-owned requestId/policy/sessionEpoch; the server publishes neither configured nomination policy nor scoped canonical request discovery. Existing findByRequest requires the unknown ID, and replay hashes include the original policy. A context GET must not call today's activity-touching requireAdminSession and cannot solve stable identity with a session ref or browser storage alone. The existing graph only returned MeResponse for the relevant query; source corroboration was required. No Graphify command or index mutation was run under root's exclusive lease.

This proposal is one read seam and one first-intent identity rule. It does not create a provider account, configure credentials, activate READY, mount the form, change the proof DTO, or implement provider onboarding. Root must approve the strict C1 addition and exact paths before source work.

### Proposed endpoint and strict public schemas

Add GET /v1/sites/:siteId/receiving/nomination-context and internalGiftsEndpointPaths.receivingNominationContext. Every success/error uses Cache-Control:no-store. Reject query strings, unexpected request body, foreign/missing trusted admin Origin, invalid site and absent/expired/revoked current session. Resolve current selected-site membership: only active SITE_ADMIN with configureReceiving, never OWNER or assistant. Do not accept user/session/operator/environment/request/policy from query, body or caller flags. No recent-auth proof is required merely to read one's context; the POST keeps its existing CONFIGURE_RECEIVING proof and locked fresh authorization.

Propose receivingNominationContextSchema and receivingNominationContextResponseSchema as strict objects/discriminated union, exported with inferred types:

- AVAILABLE: {siteId,mode,state:"AVAILABLE",requestId,policyVersion}. This is a first local intent context from the configured server scope, not an account or reservation. Both mode and policyVersion must come from the same immutable server configuration actually used by the nomination action.
- PENDING: {siteId,mode,state:"PENDING",requestId,policyVersion,currentPolicyVersion,postAllowed,nomination}. policyVersion is the original persisted intent version; currentPolicyVersion is the configured current version. nomination reuses the existing strict receivingNominationResponseSchema nomination projection (siteId/requestId/AWAITING_PROVIDER_VERIFICATION/createdAt/mode). Its site/request/mode must match the context exactly; postAllowed must equal policyVersion===currentPolicyVersion. An equal-policy POST still needs new current-session proof and the original exact request; a changed-policy pending intent is readable but cannot POST.

Do not add success DTOs with fabricated defaults when configuration or evidence is missing. Missing context/action/proof/read-only-auth configuration returns existing sanitized503 RECEIVING_NOMINATION_UNAVAILABLE; conflicts use existing409 RECIPIENT_CONFLICT/RECEIVING_BINDING_EXISTS/REQUEST_CONFLICT. Existing provider/account/credential/audit/hash/operator details, passwords and tokens never leave the server. Existing requestId is an idempotency correlation value, not a capability. No new public session credential or proof-subject field is proposed.

### Canonical discovery and deterministic first request identity

Add a separate read port to the existing repository factory, e.g. readNominationScope({siteId,operatorUserId,environment}), returning private typed scope evidence, not raw SQL rows. In one read-only transaction/snapshot, inspect the existing scoped receiving account and ACCOUNT_CREATE operation; validate current account/provider/site/environment/operator, kind/state, original request snapshot/hash/IDs and exact stored timestamp fences with readReceivingNominationSnapshot/databaseTimestamp helpers. No inserts/updates/activity refresh/advisory identity reservation/provider call. Limit cardinality and fail unavailable/conflict for ambiguous rows, missing joins, wrong operator, non-current account, existing provider binding or unsupported operation phase. Never interpret malformed evidence or another operator's candidate as an empty scope. Only the existing locally unbound UNVERIFIED candidate/REQUESTED operation is projected as AWAITING_PROVIDER_VERIFICATION; future provider phases require their own reviewed projection instead of guessing.

When a canonical candidate exists, return its original requestId and original policy before considering a derived ID, including historical arbitrary IDs. The authenticated caller must be that stored operator and still hold the selected-site role/capability. Never disclose another operator's request or audit. A stale GET grants no write authority: the existing site/account/current-session/membership transaction fences remain authoritative on POST.

When verified scope is empty, derive a non-secret deterministic identifier: nr1_ + base64url(SHA256(UTF8(JSON.stringify(["receiving.self-nomination.request.v1",siteId,operatorUserId,"ASAAS",environment])))). Use full SHA256 output and unambiguous tuple serialization. This rule excludes session/proof/policy/time/randomness, so two tabs, remount, lost ACK and a new valid session for the same operator/site/environment get the same first ID. Different operator/site/provider/environment scopes are distinct. GET computes but never persists or allocates anything. No real or synthetic secret key is required; the digest is not authentication or consent.

Export the derivation from receivingNomination.ts to avoid a new cyclic module or second pricing/auth model. In the action/service and the locked repository path, a truly new empty-scope intent must use that canonical first ID; an existing request must preserve the original ID/hash/policy/audit. Context and POST must share this rule. A client-chosen random ID cannot create another first intent. Existing nomination tests using arbitrary IDs must be adapted only for first creation; explicit legacy stored-ID replay remains covered. Concurrent different operators still arbitrate under the existing site lock: one candidate wins and the other gets conflict, never a second account.

### Policy, identity rotation, startup and session ownership

Use one copied/frozen injected configuration {mode,applicationEnvironment,policyVersion,requestIdentityVersion:"nr1"} for context and action; require matching configured action/proof/context ports, not a browser availability boolean. The current intent policy is not a fee tariff, legal/provider consent or UI test literal. No environment variable/default production policy or real key is introduced in this source slice. Live remains production-only/non-demo; unsupported mode/application combinations and missing ports fail unavailable. Existing startup index.ts continues omitting these ports, so default runtime and form remain unavailable/unmounted.

Policy rotation does not rotate requestId. Existing pending intent retains its original requestId/policy; context returns the new configured currentPolicyVersion with postAllowed:false. POST cannot upgrade old consent/audit, replace the candidate or create a new request because the UI changed policy. A new policy may govern a genuinely empty first intent, but its deterministic scope ID stays the same. Request identity derivation nr1 is fixed across deploys/sessions/policy changes; no automatic rotation, TTL or key rotation. A future derivation-version change/replacement/operator reassignment requires a separate reviewed rule and cannot bypass existing canonical records. Existing records are retained; GET never deletes or resets them. Mode changes are explicit server configuration changes, not browser choices, and cannot reinterpret a sandbox record as live.

B's reviewed session owner should use existing validated MeResponse user/session/current selected access plus its observable generation. Capture that generation for context/proof requests; quarantine results until a fresh current identity/access verification still matches it, dropping both on uncertainty/mismatch/replacement. Context reads must not poll /me or extend idle life. Explicit sensitive intent can perform the already planned /me verification without adding a new credential or parsing opaque proof tokens. The POST's server proof/session/membership fence remains authoritative for races after browser verification. B continues owning composition; A does not edit B source or mount nomination/proof under this proposal.

### Exact proposed source release, split from actual runtime/provider work

If approved, A needs these exact fifteen paths and no others:

1. New apps/api/src/receivingNominationContext.ts and .test.ts: pure context service, strict public projection, injected immutable configuration/read ports; zero side effects.
2. Existing apps/api/src/receivingNomination.ts and .test.ts: canonical non-secret first request derivation/guard while preserving legacy stored-ID replay and unchanged explicit proof/policy/locked authorization.
3. Existing apps/api/src/receivingNominationRepository.ts and .test.ts: bounded read-only scoped discovery plus same canonical first-ID guard under existing reserve locks; reuse current schema and timestamp helper, no migration.
4. Existing apps/api/src/receivingNominationHttp.ts and .test.ts: add protected no-store GET via explicit read-only admin authentication; preserve POST wire shape and origin/proof/current-membership fences.
5. Existing packages/contracts/src/internalGifts.ts, index.ts and internalGifts.test.ts: strict context union/type exports/endpoint map only; no changes to existing MeResponse, proof response, nomination POST, readiness or finance schemas.
6. Existing apps/api/src/app.ts and appIntegration.test.ts: optional receivingNominationContext port passed to the already registered router; test default unavailable and isolation from other routes. No index.ts/runtimeConfig.ts/manifests/environment file change.
7. Existing apps/api/src/authHttp.ts and authHttp.test.ts require an explicit coordinator/B handoff before A writes: minimally reuse the existing fresh cookie-cache-disabled/refresh-disabled lookup and session/membership validator for a new readAdministrativeSession export that does not touch lastActiveAt/updatedAt. Keep getAdministrativeSession/requireAdminSession and /me default activity behavior unchanged. Prefer this shared read-only branch over duplicating security-critical session logic in a finance module. These B-owned paths are not released by this proposal.

If root declines the authHttp two-path handoff, the new context may exist only as a pure/injected source port with default unavailable; do not silently use requireAdminSession and claim a write-free GET. Actual startup policy/auth/signing/action/repository composition, mounted protected browser QA and PostgreSQL acceptance require separate explicit scopes/leases after these source contracts pass review. No schema, migration, actual provider account or credential task is bundled.

### Focused RED cases before implementation

- Read-only auth: stale cookie cache, refresh suppression, expired/idle/absolute/future session, inactive/revoked user/membership, OWNER/assistant/foreign site; context never updates/inserts/deletes any row or refreshes cookies, while existing /me behavior remains unchanged.
- HTTP/defaults: missing/foreign Origin, query/body injection, absent ports/configuration, invalid policy/mode/environment/demo, malformed private rows, strict response extras, wrong same-site request/mode/nomination, sanitized no-store failures; no action/proof/provider port invocation during GET.
- Identity: repeat/refetch/remount/two tabs/new session share first ID; scope distinctions; policy changes leave first ID unchanged; no random/time/proof dependence, no DB write and no actual account creation from GET.
- Discovery: one valid current self candidate returns exact original ID/policy/createdAt, including legacy ID; another operator or provider-bound/non-current/ambiguous/orphan/unsupported-phase rows fail closed and never become AVAILABLE. Native Date/raw PostgreSQL strings retain microsecond fences before public milliseconds.
- Action/races: new random ID denied before inserts; canonical winner accepted UNVERIFIED/REQUESTED only; identical concurrent calls/replay/lost acknowledgment keep one account/operation; changed policy exposes original PENDING/postAllowed:false and rejects POST without writes/new ID; existing proof freshness/session replacement/revocation fences remain intact.
- Privacy: DTO/request derivation reveals no account/provider credential/audit/password/token; scope hash grants no action authority. B's separate tests cover stale generation/context/proof settlement and quarantine, without A editing its files.

Proposed validation is explicit non-DB source/unit suites, API source TypeScript, scoped Biome/diff, and independent Sol logic/security review. No tests or implementation for this proposal have run; the proposal must not be reported as an implemented context, mounted form or operational finance flow.

PIX includes fixed and percentage fee, minimum/maximum caps, discount expiry and monthly free/received counters; credit card includes operation fixed fee and per-installment percentages/promotions; transfer includes monthly free quota, PIX/TED inclusion flags, amounts and discount expiry.
